  • Against the margin:
  • OCCAR dilution: about -0.6 to -0.8 points on night vision from 2027, as OCCAR rises to about 55% to 60% of night-vision revenue. Deutsche Bank cut EBIT 3% on it.
  • Platform entry at about 20% (Kappa; Deutsche Bank, 1 October 2025).
  • Wage localisation and the Athens and Belgian fixed costs.
  • A negative geographic mix: Europe was 80% of H1 2026 revenue against 75% in FY2025, and export gross margins are about 30% against about 20% in Europe (expert, 1 April 2026).
  • For the margin: HGH (about +0.6 to +0.8 points from 2027); thermal and digital lines with no tube (over 40% gross margin; IRIS-C); Harder's yield gains; and the purchasing scale of a group three times its FY2023 size.
  • The expert claims of a 15% to 18% EBIT margin (former head of procurement, 11 March 2026; former regional manager, 1 April 2026) are contradicted by every print since: 26.2% in H1 2026, Kappa ahead of plan, and guidance raised. I reject them for the base case. They are a correct statement of the direction of the fixed-cost drift, not of its size.

4. How the operating leverage shapes the cone (for Set 3).

  • Downside, FY2027: a 10% revenue shortfall (about 75 EURm, for example an OCCAR tranche or option slip of a quarter) leaves about 11% of revenue in fixed cost that cannot flex within the year. EBIT falls by 11% to 13%. With fixed interest, adjusted EPS falls by about 15% (1.68 to about 1.43 EUR), and leverage rises about 0.3x.
  • Upside, FY2027: a 5% beat earns an incremental margin of about 32% to 35% (gross margin less variable opex). EBIT rises about 6% and EPS about 7%. But night vision cannot supply it beyond tube allocation, so the beat must come from thermal, platforms or HGH.
  • Within the year: a Q4 slip of 10% of Q4 revenue (about 25 to 27 EURm) moves the full-year margin by about 0.5 to 0.7 points, because Q4 carries the absorption.

5. What it means line by line, against history.

  • Revenue: growth slows from a 52% compound rate (FY2020 to FY2025) to 24% (FY2025 to FY2028) and 18% (FY2025 to FY2030).
  • Gross margin: about 34% to 35% (35.1% in H1 2026; 32.6% in FY2025, FactSet). It rises slowly as non-night-vision mix rises, but OCCAR holds it below the 37.9% of Q2 2026.
  • Opex intensity: rises about 0.5 to 1 point of revenue by FY2028 as the fixed block is built ahead of the non-night-vision revenue.
  • EBIT margin: flat at about 26.4% (company basis), against a fall of eight points over FY2021 to FY2025 (FactSet basis).
  • EBIT growth therefore equals revenue growth: about 24.7% a year over FY2025 to FY2028 (116.1 to 225 EURm).

6. Peers.

  • MilDef, a framework assembler, holds its margin on mix (49% to 50% gross margin on its own products against 30% to 32% at its Roda distribution arm) and on contracts that protect its gross margin from component prices.
  • Hensoldt's Optronics EBITDA margin rose from 1.3% to 12.2% (Q1 2025 to Q1 2026) on real fixed-cost leverage, from a far lower base.
  • Theon sits between them: MilDef's variable cost structure, at a higher margin level.
5c

Financial leverage

2 answers
C-7635P-0238
C-76How material is the financial leverage or gearing: how much does it amplify earnings per share and risk, and how comfortable is it?F2829th of 30full answer under 4c Financial profile
P-02

After assessing the financial leverage in greater detail, how is it likely to shape earnings per share and the balance sheet over the next 18 months to four years?

F4225th of 30

Layer 2 · Financial forecasts

Judgement: financial leverage is the largest single amplifier of EPS outcomes in the 18-month window and the main reason EPS grows slower than EBIT until FY2027. From FY2028 the same mechanism runs in reverse.

  • Net debt peaks at about 2.9x to 3.0x EBITDA in H1 2027, when the HGH cash goes out. That leaves about 18% of EBITDA as cushion to the 3.5x covenant.
  • The interest bill goes from about 7 EURm (FY2025 net, company basis) to about 25 EURm (FY2027). That costs about 0.14 EUR a share, or 8% of FY2027 EPS, against a no-debt counterfactual.
  • Net debt falls to about 2.0x at end-2028 and 1.2x at end-2030, adding about 1 to 2 points a year to EPS growth over FY2028 to FY2030.
  • The base case meets the company's "below 2.5x in 2027" target only narrowly (2.44x). The H1 2027 report is therefore the binary event for the multiple.

1. The net-debt path (company definition, EURm; leverage on the year's EBITDA, adjusted EBIT plus depreciation and amortisation).

End-2025 Jun-2026 End-2026 Q1 2027, pro forma for HGH Mid-2027 peak End-2027 End-2028 End-2029 End-2030
Net debt (cash) (126.9) 237.9 about 250 about 540 about 575 about 515 about 475 about 420 about 350
Net debt / EBITDA net cash 1.7x 1.5x about 2.9x (pro forma EBITDA about 189) about 2.9x–3.0x 2.44x 2.0x 1.6x 1.2x
  • The H2 2026 movements:
  • free cash flow of only about 14 EURm in H2, because Q4 invoicing is collected in Q1;
  • Merio for about 22 EURm (closed 29 October 2026);
  • about 10 EURm of small stakes (Varjo; timing not disclosed);
  • incentive-plan share purchases.
  • The 2027 movements:
  • HGH for about 290 EURm of cash (Q1 2027) and about 5 EURm of bolt-ons;
  • the FY2026 dividend of about 25 EURm;
  • free cash flow of about 60 EURm after interest, tax, capital spending and leases.
  • The record argues for leaning net debt worse than the company: net debt was 18% worse than consensus at Q1 2026 and 8.8% worse than Deutsche Bank at H1 2026.
  • Two conflicts between sources:
  • Company net debt of 237.9 EURm at 30 June 2026 against FactSet's 213 EURm. Use the company figure for the covenant.
  • Deutsche Bank's 573 EURm and 3.4x at end-2026 assumed HGH closing in 2026. It is the same path, one quarter earlier.

2. Covenant arithmetic at the peak. At about 575 EURm of net debt in mid-2027, a breach of 3.5x needs LTM EBITDA below about 164 EURm, against about 197 EURm pro forma. That is a cushion of about 17%.

  • The cushion is larger if the bank definition nets the Exosens stake (283 EURm at 56.9 EUR). Bernstein computes underlying leverage at about 1.5x on that basis. But the definition is not disclosed.
  • The first-set risk of a covenant-forced equity raise (about 10% inside 18 months) needs a combination: OCCAR or option slippage of 15% or more, a Q4 2026 or Q4 2027 collection slip, and no stake sale. I keep 10%.

3. The interest bill (net financial items, excluding the Exosens marks, including the Exosens dividend).

EURm FY2025 (actual, company basis) FY2026 FY2027 FY2028 FY2029 FY2030
Net financial items -6.8 -18 -25 -23 -20 -16
Average net debt (approximately) net cash about 245 about 540 about 525 about 470 about 400
  • The build:
  • about 4.2% to 4.6% all-in on average net debt: Euribor about 2% plus 1.5% on the 300 EURm revolving facility to 2030, the 325 EURm five-year term loan (7 September 2026) on slightly better terms, fees, and cash earning less than debt costs;
  • plus about 4 EURm a year of other finance costs (FX losses were 2.04 EURm in H1 2025; bank guarantees; arrangement-fee amortisation);
  • less the Exosens dividend of about 3 to 5 EURm.
  • Sensitivity: +100 basis points on the floating cost is about 5.5 EURm in FY2027, or 0.05 EUR a share (-3%). No interest-rate swaps are disclosed. The company's own sensitivity (0.9 EURm per 100 basis points) is on the FY2025 base and is now stale.
  • Against consensus: consensus FY2027 EBIT less pre-tax profit (199 less 179 EURm) is about 20 EURm. Brokers carry about 5 EURm less interest than I do in FY2027, which is about 0.05 EUR a share.

4. How leverage shapes EPS.

  • Adjusted net income over adjusted EBIT: 0.71 (FY2025), 0.67 (FY2026), 0.65 (FY2027), 0.67 (FY2028), 0.68 (FY2029), 0.69 (FY2030).
  • Adjusted EPS growth against adjusted EBIT growth:
  • FY2027: +21% against +25%.
  • FY2028: +15% against +13%. Deleveraging has started to add.
  • FY2029: +13% against +11%.
  • The amplifier at the peak: a 10% EBITDA miss in FY2027 is about a 15% EPS miss and adds about 0.3x of leverage, which delays the sub-2.5x print by about a year.
  • The December 2025 rights issue (8.6m shares at 17.40 EUR, +12% to the count) is already in the FY2026 base, so dilution does not recur in the forecast.

5. The balance sheet.

  • HGH adds about 260 to 280 EURm of goodwill and intangibles. Post-tax ROCE falls from about 31% (FY2024) to about 14% (FY2027) and recovers slowly, because the 283 EURm Exosens stake and the HGH goodwill earn below the organic base. Consensus equity rises from 497 EURm (FY2026) to 728 EURm (FY2028).
  • The related-party loans (82.66 EURm, interest-free, a KPMG key audit matter; related-party receivables of 23.78 EURm at June 2026 against 8.93 EURm at December 2025) are a cash claim that does not earn. I model no recovery and no loss.

6. The Exosens stake is the one lever that changes the path, and it cuts both ways.

  • A sale at 56.9 EUR raises about 283 EURm, against a cost of 268.7 EURm.
  • Leverage falls about 1.3x (end-2027 to about 1.1x).
  • Net interest falls about 13 EURm, less the dividend of about 4 EURm forgone, so about +9 EURm pre-tax: +0.09 EUR a share (+4% to +5% on FY2028).
  • The sale removes the reported-EPS volatility and almost certainly ends the covenant question.
  • The cost: management argued the stake supports supply coordination. But the supply agreement runs to 2030 on its own, and the board-seat plan was dropped in September 2026, so the strategic case for holding is now weak.
  • I put the probability of a sale or monetisation inside 18 months at about 30%. I do not include it in the base, because management has not said it will sell.
  • If the stake is held and Exosens de-rates: at its 56th percentile the stock can fall 20% to 30%. That is a 57 to 85 EURm reported loss with no cash effect, but it would weaken any covenant netting argument.
5d

Cost-out scope NTM

2 answers
C-7745P-0345
C-77

How much of each major part of the cost base is cuttable or reducible?

F3522nd of 30

Layer 1 · Company - Economic strength

The Investment Analyst's heading Cuttable costs: how much of each major part of the cost base is cuttable or reducible?

Judgement: little of the P&L is cuttable in the classical sense. The levers are working capital, capex phasing and M&A spend, which matter more to cash and EPS than cost cuts.

  • Tubes and components (about 60% to 65% of COGS): not cuttable, only pass-through and dual-sourcing.
  • Assembly labour: variable and flexes with volume; Greek wage inflation is the drift.
  • Opex (about 9% of revenue): modest; acquisition synergies come through common purchasing and integrated R&D, and are unquantified.
  • R&D (2.8% of revenue): should not be cut.
  • Discretionary: capex of about 30 to 37 EURm a year and further M&A.
P-03

After assessing the scope for cost elimination and efficiency in greater detail, how much is likely to reach the main financial line items over the next 18 months to four years, and when?

D4517th of 30

Layer 2 · Financial forecasts

Judgement: almost none of it reaches the EBIT margin as a net gain. The identifiable cost efficiencies are about 5 to 8 EURm a year by FY2029, and wage localisation, new plants and integration consume them. They are one reason the margin holds at about 26.4% rather than falling. The efficiency that does reach the financial lines is cash: working capital and the integration costs ending.

  • Working capital falling from about 42.5% of revenue (end-2026) to 38.5% (FY2030) releases about 35 to 40 EURm against a constant ratio, nearly all of it from FY2028.
  • The non-recurring costs fall from about 8.5 EURm (FY2026) to about 4 EURm (FY2027), 2 EURm (FY2028) and nil.

1. What is cuttable and what is not.

  • Tubes and components (about 60% to 65% of night-vision cost): not cuttable. The levers are dual sourcing (Harder) and multi-year fixed prices.
  • Assembly labour: variable. The drift is upward: Greek wages, and German, Belgian and Gulf localisation.
  • Opex (about 9% of revenue): modest scope. No cost programme exists, and no cost-synergy figure has been given. The only synergy target is 30 EURm of revenue synergies by 2029.
  • R&D (2.8% of revenue): should rise, not fall, for a company entering software-defined sensing.

2. The identifiable efficiencies, sized (my estimates).

Source Mechanism Size by FY2029 When
Group purchasing (optics, electronics, housings) across seven business units volume discounts, fewer suppliers about 2 to 3 EURm from 2028
Harder tubes replacing Exosens tubes on non-NATO lines (about 13,000 a year, about 66% internal) margin capture on the tube, if Harder's yield holds about 2 to 4 EURm 2027 to 2028, as capacity reaches 20,000
Shared R&D and engineering (Kappa, Merio, HGH, ShockEOS) avoided duplicate hiring about 1 to 2 EURm 2028 onward
Total about 5 to 8 EURm, about 0.5 to 0.8 points of FY2029 revenue mostly 2028 to 2029
  • Offsets, of a similar size: wage inflation of 3% to 5% a year on a payroll that rose 10.87 EURm in H1 2026 alone; the Athens and Belgian fixed costs; and Western-European cost rigidity on shifts and overtime (expert, 13 April 2026).
  • Net margin effect: about nil. I put none of it in as margin expansion.

3. The precedents say a company of Theon's size and structure should not be credited with a cost programme.

  • The precedents that worked had large, identifiable overheads to cut:
  • L3Harris's LHX NeXt: 1.4 USDbn of run-rate savings against a 1 USDbn target, from a 5% workforce cut and sites reduced from 275 to about 200.
  • Thales's COVID plan: 850 EURm against a 750 EURm target, half from procurement.
  • Hensoldt–ESG: 19 EURm of run-rate cost synergies by 2028, for 8 to 10 EURm of integration cost.
  • Theon has none of these: a lean founder-run overhead, one main plant cluster and no duplicated head offices.
  • The relevant precedent is Exail's refinancing: 4% convertible bonds replacing a 12% ICG instrument, cutting borrowing costs about 40%. For Theon the equivalent is an Exosens stake sale (P-02), which is worth about 9 EURm pre-tax a year, more than any cost lever.

4. The cash efficiencies that do reach the lines.

  • Working capital:
  • Net working capital was 41.6% of last-twelve-month revenue at June 2026 (FactSet), against a company target of 35%, MilDef's 31.2% (September 2026) and Renk's about 25%.
  • I model 42.5% (end-2026, with the Q4 receivables peak), 41%, 40%, 39%, 38.5% (FY2030).
  • Each point is about 7.5 EURm at FY2027 revenue and about 10 EURm at FY2030 revenue. Each 10 EURm of debt avoided is about 0.5 EURm of interest a year.
  • Why only to 38.5%, not 35%:
    • part of the working capital is the price of tube priority (stockpiles, prompt supplier payment);
    • OCCAR receivables sit in the Hensoldt–Theon joint venture (34.95 EURm owed at end-2025);
    • no customer prepayments are visible, unlike Hensoldt's about 15% or Rheinmetall's about 30%.
  • Reaching 35% by FY2029 would release a further about 38 EURm and take end-2029 leverage to about 1.4x. That is the main cash upside.
  • Capital spending: 31 EURm (FY2026, against the 30 EURm budget), 36, 38, 42 and 45 EURm, about 4.4% to 4.8% of revenue. There is no efficiency here. Capex is rising because the plants are rising, and it is not discretionary while backlog conversion depends on capacity.
  • Non-recurring costs (below adjusted EBIT): about 8.5 EURm in FY2026 (3.89 EURm in H1, plus Merio and HGH deal costs), about 4 EURm in FY2027 (HGH integration), about 2 EURm in FY2028.

5. Timing for the Consumer.

  • Inside 18 months: nothing measurable reaches the margin. The cash lines get worse before they get better.
  • 18 months to four years: the working-capital ratio and the end of the integration costs are the measurable gains. Together they add about 0.03 to 0.05 EUR a share by FY2029 through lower interest, and roughly halve the time to 1.5x leverage against a flat-ratio case.
5e

Reinforcing feedback loops

2 answers
C-4760P-0550
C-47To what extent do gravitational or mechanical forces, rather than heroic company dynamism, improve the company's competitive advantage and its financial outputs against peers with each period that passes?B607th of 30full answer under 4a Business quality
P-05

After assessing in greater detail whether reinforcing or balancing feedback loops are at work in the business model, such that dynamics at play today create additional scope for further positive/negative dynamics in the near/mid-term (reinforcing feedback loops), or the opposite (balancing loops), how are they likely to shape the main financial line items over the next 18 months to four years?

D4618th of 30

Layer 2 · Financial forecasts

Judgement: two regimes, with the switch in 2028.

  • FY2026 to FY2027: balancing loops dominate the lines that reach the shareholder.
  • Growth absorbs working capital, which raises net debt and interest, which caps EPS and M&A. Deutsche Bank cut FY2027 free-cash-flow conversion to 39% from 49%.
  • A leverage and de-rating loop runs negative on the multiple: leverage leads to de-rating, which makes equity expensive, which pushes funding to debt.
  • The revenue loops reinforce (installed base, tube priority) but hit the tube cap, so they extend the backlog's duration rather than lifting annual revenue.
  • From FY2028 the same growth-and-working-capital loop flips. Slower growth absorbs less cash, so net debt falls, interest falls and EPS grows faster than EBIT. That creates a reinforcing positive loop on EPS and, if the multiple follows, on the cost of capital.
  • Behind both regimes, a slow balancing loop acts on night-vision volume. Exosens is diversifying away from Theon (its own downstream goggles and the US BiNOD programme), which caps Theon's tube allocation after 2028.

The loops, one by one.

Loop Type Operating now? (evidence, dated) Dominant when How it shows in the lines
L1. Installed base and qualification lock-in, leading to the next frameworks and modular upgrades (clip-ons, fusion) Reinforcing Yes. About 300,000 systems in service; OCCAR adds 100,000 binoculars; IRIS-C clip-ons for OCCAR IRCOD (50 EURm initial order plus 150 EURm options, September 2025); ORION fused goggles' first Western-European order (30 September 2026) 2027 to 2030 Supports non-night-vision organic growth of 20% to 26% a year and its gross margin above 40%; makes the 2029–2030 replacement cycle Theon's to lose
L2. Being Exosens' largest buyer: priority allocation, then the ability to fulfil the largest frameworks, then a larger share of Exosens output Reinforcing, but bounded Yes. 400,000-plus tubes over 2026–2030, 52% of Exosens' projected output (Bernstein, 4 December 2025); 18-month lead times for rivals 2026 to 2028 Keeps European night-vision share at 50% to 60% and guarantees OCCAR supply; but the bound is Exosens' capacity, so night-vision revenue grows at tube-volume-plus-price, 4% to 11% a year
L3. Exosens' dependence on Theon (40.6% of its revenue), leading it to diversify: NVLS (17,000 Spanish monoculars), US BiNOD (up to 352.6 USDm, March 2026), Sturbridge US capacity Balancing, against Theon Yes, and strengthening 2028 onward Caps Theon's allocation growth after OCCAR; caps night vision at about 4% to 5% a year in FY2029–FY2030; raises the stakes of the 2030 supply renewal
L4. But NATO buyers avoid Harder tubes ("Tier-2"; Serbian and Russian legacy) 2027 to 2030 About 13,000 internal tubes for non-NATO lines; third-party revenue toward about 25 EURm by 2028 (Deutsche Bank: Harder about 50 EURm of revenue by 2028); a hedge, not a substitute
L5. Revenue growth absorbing working capital (about 41% to 42% of revenue), raising net debt and interest, limiting M&A and equity value Balancing Yes, the dominant loop now. Net debt from 126.9 EURm net cash to 237.9 EURm net debt in six months; interest expense +158% in H1 2026 2026 to 2027; reverses from 2028 Working-capital absorption of about 48 EURm (FY2026) and 41 EURm (FY2027); adjusted net income over EBIT falls to 0.65 (FY2027); then absorption falls to about 27 EURm a year and the ratio recovers to 0.69 (FY2030)
L6. Agency buying consolidation, raising volume and buyer power together Balancing on margin Yes. OCCAR is the proof; agency share expected to rise from about 20% to about 40% by 2030 (Deutsche Bank) 2027 to 2029 Night-vision margin -0.6 to -0.8 points in FY2027; customer concentration persistent at 35% to 40%
L7. Localisation required by content rules: orders won, but wage cost rises Balancing on margin Yes. Zaventem (March 2026); the joint venture tripling its German staff; Saudi 50% local content; Al Meezan 2026 to 2030 The fixed block rises; absorbs the cost efficiencies in P-03; margin flat rather than up
L8. Share-price de-rating raising the cost of equity-funded growth, pushing to debt, raising leverage, de-rating further Reinforcing, negative Yes. Theon -13.7% in the three months to 28 October 2026 while its electronics peers held; the 325 EURm term loan chosen over equity Until the H1 2027 print or a stake sale breaks it Multiple, not earnings; but it raises the odds that the next deal is debt-funded, and of a discounted raise in stress
L9. Deleveraging lowering interest, raising EPS growth, re-rating, lowering the cost of capital Reinforcing, positive Not yet From 2028 (from mid-2027 if the stake is sold) Adds about 1 to 2 points a year to EPS growth over FY2028–FY2030; dividend growth (0.45 to 0.60 EUR)

What the loops together do to each line.

  • Revenue: L1 and L2 reinforce, but L2 is bounded by Exosens' capacity and L3 tightens that bound over time. So the backlog lengthens rather than revenue accelerating: options (902 EURm) and pre-selections add years, not annual volume. Group growth after FY2027 is set by how fast L1 converts into non-night-vision revenue.
  • Gross and EBIT margin: L6 and L7 (negative) against L1 (thermal and digital mix) and L4 (positive). Net flat.
  • Working capital and net debt: L5 dominates to end-2027, then reverses into L9. The turn needs growth to slow to about 13% (FY2028) and the working-capital ratio to edge down. It does not need a working-capital programme. This is the main finding: the cash story improves mechanically as growth slows, which makes FY2028 cash more predictable than FY2027 cash.
  • EPS: suppressed by L5 and L8 through FY2027 (EPS +21% against EBIT +25%), then lifted by L9 (EPS +15% against EBIT +13% in FY2028).

What would strengthen, stall or reverse the loops.

  • An Exosens stake sale breaks L8 and starts L9 about a year early.
  • A Q4 collection slip, or a large new debt-funded acquisition, extends L5 and L8 into 2028.
  • A ceasefire would weaken L1's replacement pull in 2029 and 2030 and intensify L6 (price wars).
  • Exosens yield or capacity trouble (OCCAR plus BiNOD plus the Sturbridge transfer) would turn L2 into a revenue-timing problem in 2027.
  • Customer prepayments on new frames, Hensoldt's loop (about 15% of order value), would weaken L5 at once. There is no evidence that Theon has them.

The peers show each loop's end-state.

  • MilDef shows L5 reversing: net working capital 38.9% to 31.2% of sales, net debt over EBITDA 1.9x to 0.7x in a year.
  • Hensoldt shows prepayments neutralising L5.
  • Exail shows L9 driven by refinancing (12% to 4%).
  • Elbit shows full tube integration: after buying Harris Night Vision for 350 USDm (2019) it is sole prime on ENVG-B, the end-state that L4 cannot reach for Theon in Europe.

Current guidance and SS management, simple case and no SHMLP risks (and comparable track record)

B+754th of 30

Memo topic 4, Conservatism of guidance and record of delivery: the Rater's score

6a

Guidance conservatism

A723rd of 303 answers
Judgement62C-6855C-7165
Group judgement

Guidance

A723rd of 30

Layer 1 · Guidance

Written last by the Investment Analyst for its group

Guidance is conservative, specific on operations and reliable, and the record is a short but unbroken sequence of beat-and-raise.

  • For FY2026, guidance and consensus coincide, with modest margin upside.
  • For FY2027, consensus sits on the medium-term framework plus acquisitions.
  • The guidance is silent on exactly the lines where the case's risk lives: interest, cash conversion, the covenant definition, and OCCAR's margin.
  • For Set 3: high confidence in revenue and EBIT guidance; low confidence in any implied EPS and net-debt path.
C-68

To what extent is company guidance above or below sell-side consensus for this financial year and next financial year, and why?

C-73

What else is material or uncommon about the company's guidance or investor relations?

C-4212th of 30

Layer 1 · Guidance

Judgement: five items that a model and a market narrative must handle.

  1. "Soft backlog" includes framework awards not yet called off. Options are separate (902 EURm), and the company states a 100% historic exercise record.
  2. Reported EPS carries Exosens fair-value marks every period, and brokers treat them inconsistently: FY2026 EPS ranges from 1.28 to 1.95 EUR.
  3. Seasonality: historically about 70% of sales fell in H2 (FY2025 actual about 59%), and Q4 carries most of the year's earnings.
  4. The listed history is 2.6 years. Cypriot law constrained the Capital Markets Day disclosures. A new IR representative starts late 2026.
  5. Seven visible brokers, all Buy (100% buy share, up 11 points over three months), and no dissenting voice. It is a thin, uniform coverage, so a single downgrade would carry outsized weight.

Group judgement

Risks

C5411th of 30

Layer 3 · Risks

Written last by the Investment Analyst for its group

The forecasts are predictable in two tiers, and the tiers are inverted against what the market is pricing. Revenue and adjusted EBIT through FY2027 rest on demonstrated strengths (a contracted, supply-paced backlog; a margin held at 26% for three years; a beat-and-raise record), are disclosed quarterly in the company's own definitions, and move in a cone of about ±8% on revenue and ±1 point on margin with a downside skew that has a documented base rate (one in three German call-offs slips a quarter). Earnings per share, net debt and free cash flow rest on strengths not yet shown (releasing working capital while growing, deleveraging from a debt-funded peak, integrating four units at once), are disclosed twice a year in definitions that differ from FactSet's, and move in a cone that is wide (FY2027 adjusted EPS about 1.35 to 1.78 EUR around 1.63; end-2027 leverage 1.1x to 3.0x) and bimodal on the balance sheet, where the largest swing is a management decision on the Exosens stake.

The answers to the earlier sets are congruent on all of this, and the places where they jar (the Exosens dividend, the tax rate, the amortisation inside adjusted EBIT, the covenant level) are all in the second tier. This set resolves two of them by changing inputs (tax to 25%, net financial items heavier), which lowers adjusted EPS by about 3% to 1.35, 1.63 and 1.87 EUR for FY2026 to FY2028, and leaves two open (the covenant level and the adjusted-EBIT definition) as research and monitoring items.

The drivers are concentrated and correlated: German and OCCAR call-off timing on a fixed tube supply, the working-capital ratio and the HGH contribution against its debt explain most of FY2027's variance, and a ceasefire or a budget delay would move all three in the same half-year as the leverage peak. That makes the case easy to monitor and impossible to diversify from inside, and it makes the mid-2027 covenant test (cushion about 16% of EBITDA at 3.5x, about 2% if the revolving facility's 3.0x still applies) the one place where a timing problem becomes a shareholder loss. The Exosens stake, liquid and tax-free, converts most of that tail into a de-risking event rather than a dilution; the exception is a correlated sector shock that marks the stake down at the same time.

The share price and the multiple are three-quarters company-driven, so they will respond to Theon's own prints: the end-2026 net debt (19 March 2027) and the June 2027 leverage (September 2027), the second of which falls outside the six-month monitoring window and near the end of the 18-month one. Until then the operating prints, which are the predictable ones, are the ones the market has said it no longer pays for.

For the memo: the predictability of the financial forecasts over the next 18 months is medium overall, high for the lines that are not in dispute and low for the lines that decide the share price, and the resolution of the second tier is dated: 19 March 2027 for the accounting ambiguities, September 2027 for the leverage, and any day for the stake. A case built on the operating forecasts alone would score well above 50; a case built on the balance-sheet forecasts alone would score well below; the investment case needs both.

D-02

To what extent does past and present fundamental delivery make the expected change plausible, and to what extent does the thesis rely only on strengths the business demonstrably has, rather than on strengths it would need to acquire?

F5527th of 30

Layer 3 · Risks

Judgement: the operating half of the expected change (revenue to about 750 EURm in FY2027 at a 26% to 26.5% margin) rests on strengths Theon has demonstrated for 2.6 listed years and 28 unlisted ones; the financial half (cash conversion, deleveraging from a debt-funded peak) and the post-2027 growth (tube-free lines, platforms, four integrations at once) rest on strengths it has not yet shown. The base case is plausible because it asks little of the undemonstrated strengths; the company's own targets ask a great deal of them.

The expected change, split by the strength it needs (the base case is the second set's, with this set's adjustments):

Component of the change Needs Demonstrated? Evidence, dated
FY2026 revenue 605 EURm (+36%), FY2027 752 EURm (+24%) converting a scheduled backlog; option exercise; Q4 delivery Yes FY2024 and FY2025 delivered above the top of guidance; FY2026 raised twice; 100% option-exercise record; about 500 EURm scheduled for FY2027 (H1 call, 8 September 2026); 270,000 systems delivered to end-2025
Night-vision volume of about 95,000 to 100,000 tubes a year in 2027 to 2028, OCCAR about 33,000 binoculars a year tube allocation; assembly capacity in Athens and Wetzlar Largely 400,000-plus tubes contracted to 2030 (3 December 2025); Exosens' capacity ahead of plan and guidance raised (17 September and 27 October 2026); earlier OCCAR tranches (66,000 and 8,000 units) delivered; Wetzlar tripling staff. The former regional manager's "cannot deliver 100,000 by 2027" argues against a schedule nobody holds (Deutsche Bank: one third by end-2027)
Adjusted EBIT margin 26% to 26.5% through FY2028 cost control; mix arriving on time; holding margin while localising and while OCCAR volume rises Partly 25.8%, 26.2%, 26.2% delivered (FY2024, FY2025, H1 2026); 25% in the seasonally weak Q1 2026; but no OCCAR bulk year yet, Zaventem and the Athens plant not yet absorbed, and the "anti-leverage" of larger contracts cost eight points of margin over FY2021 to FY2025
Non-night-vision organic growth of 26%, 25%, 20% selling thermal, fused and platform products into contested markets Partly non-night-vision 24% of Q2 2026 revenue against 3% a year earlier, but Kappa's acquired 41 EURm is most of it; IRIS-C (50 EURm plus 150 EURm options), PHYLAX (over 40 EURm), the 42 EURm SAFE order and ORION's first NATO order are real but young; thermal was 27 EURm in both FY2024 and FY2025 (FactSet), so organic thermal growth has not yet shown in the annual lines
Acquired revenue of 55 to 60 EURm in FY2027; HGH at a 37% margin integrating four units in four countries at once Partly Harder: capacity tripled, figure of merit 1,800 to 2,000, a 25 EURm third-party order; Kappa: orders equal to a year's revenue within five months, ahead of plan; Merio: a 5 EURm order on day one. Against: HGH is four times Kappa's size, French, software-heavy, bought at 18x, and its close has already slipped a quarter; the team is Greek and homogeneous; the CFO has not consolidated a multinational group
Working capital from 42.5% to 40% of revenue by FY2028 (base), 35% (company target) releasing cash while growing above 15% No the only improvement on record is 44.2% to 41.0% to 40.0% (FY2024, FY2025, H1 2026) during growth; stockpiling for priority and prompt supplier payment are deliberate; no prepayments; sovereign terms resist factoring
Net debt from about 2.9x (mid-2027) to below 2.5x (end-2027) and 2.0x (end-2028) deleveraging from an acquisition peak out of operating cash flow No never done; the company has been net cash or lightly geared until January 2026; net debt has printed worse than consensus at both 2026 reporting dates
Revenue of 1 EURbn and 50% non-night-vision by 2029 (company) all of the above plus the tube-free lines doubling twice No my arithmetic reaches 946 EURm and 38% in FY2029

What the precedents say about the undemonstrated strengths (template report D-1, with my reading):

  • Deleveraging after an acquisition peak has been done quickly by the closest peer: MilDef's leverage peaked at 2.6x to 2.9x in 2025 after Roda and fell to 2.0x within four quarters and 1.0x within six, while working capital fell from 41.2% to 31.2% of sales as sales grew 72%. The distinguishing features were a framework business with a distribution arm of lower working-capital intensity, prepayments on German call-offs, and no further deals. Exail deleveraged by refinancing 12% mezzanine with 4% convertibles, not from cash flow. Teledyne's 4.0x after FLIR took years. Hensoldt's post-ESG integration succeeded on a structured Day 1 to Day 200 plan, which Theon has not described.
  • Releasing working capital while growing is the step with the thinnest precedent among assemblers of scarce components: MilDef did it, but Deutsche Bank expects Theon to stay near 38% through 2028 because stockpiling is the price of priority. My base needs only 40% by FY2028, which is why I rate the base plausible and the 35% target not.
  • Integrating several acquisitions at once from a founder-led centre has a mixed record in the sector (Exail's ECA and iXblue merger carried a minority dispute and a financial overhang for years; Hensoldt's ESG worked). Theon's evidence is two small integrations that went well and one large one that has not started.

The two tests that decide whether the thesis stays on demonstrated ground:

  1. The H2 2026 margin as the first OCCAR tranches ship and Zaventem opens. If the FY2026 margin lands at 26.4% or better with Q4 deliveries on time, the "hold the margin while localising" strength moves from asserted to shown.
  2. The end-2026 net debt against about 250 EURm. A third consecutive worse-than-consensus print would mean the working-capital strength is not emerging on schedule, before HGH's 290 EURm goes out.

Why this matters more here than for most companies: the market has stopped paying for the demonstrated strength (orders, C-84) and now pays for the undemonstrated one (cash). The plausibility of the operating forecasts is high and of the financial forecasts is medium-to-low, and the share price is attached to the second.

D-04

How wide is the cone of plausible outcomes over the next 18 months, how skewed is it, and so how predictable is the case?

D4218th of 30

Layer 3 · Risks

Judgement: narrow for revenue and EBIT, wide for earnings per share and net debt, and skewed in two different directions at once. On operations the cone is skewed down, because volume upside is capped by tubes and downside is not. On the balance sheet the cone is bimodal rather than skewed: a stake sale (about 30%) puts end-2027 leverage near 1.1x, no sale leaves it at 2.3x to 2.8x, and a covenant squeeze (about 10% to 20% depending on the covenant level) forces one of the two. The case is therefore predictable in its operating lines and event-driven in the lines the market is pricing, which is the least comfortable combination for an investor: the fundamentals one can forecast are not the ones that move the stock inside 18 months.

The cone, line by line (the 18 months run to about the end of April 2028, so they take in the Q3 2026 update, the FY2026 results, the whole of FY2027 and the FY2027 results in March 2028):

Line Bear (about 20%) Base (about 60%) Bull (about 20%) Width and skew
FY2026 revenue 595 EURm (a Q4 slip of a few deliveries) 605 615 ±2%; symmetric; six weeks of Q4 decide it
FY2026 adjusted EBIT margin 25.8% 26.4% 26.9% ±0.5 points; the Q4 absorption and any pre-opening costs at Zaventem
FY2026 adjusted EPS 1.28 EUR (tax near 28% for the year; H2 finance cost heavier) 1.35 1.40 ±5%; skewed down by the tax question
FY2027 revenue 690 EURm (one OCCAR or option quarter slips; HGH late) 752 770 (non-night-vision converts faster; HGH on time) -8% / +2%; skewed down
FY2027 adjusted EBIT margin 25.3% (fixed block committed; OCCAR mix heavier) 26.5% 27.0% -1.2 / +0.5 points; skewed down
FY2027 adjusted EPS 1.35 EUR (revenue -8%, margin -1 point, rates +100 basis points); 1.25 with a discounted raise (tail, about 10%) 1.63 1.78 (operations) to 1.80 (with a stake sale from mid-2027) -17% / +10%; skewed down on operations
FY2027 reported EPS 1.15 EUR (Exosens -20%; non-recurring costs higher) 1.49 1.85 (Exosens +20%) ±25%; set by Exosens' share price, not by Theon
End-2027 net debt / EBITDA 2.8x to 3.0x (no sale) 2.48x 2.2x (no sale); about 1.1x (sale) bimodal; the sale decision is worth 1.3x
Mid-2027 peak against a 3.5x covenant (3.0x if the revolving facility's covenant still applies) 3.2x to 3.3x (a quarter's slip plus a collection slip) 2.9x 2.7x the cushion is 16% of EBITDA at 3.5x and about 2% at 3.0x
FY2027 free cash flow (FactSet definition) 35 EURm 63 85 (consensus) -45% / +35%; skewed down against consensus, roughly symmetric against my base
Share price at 14.8x (the guideline) on 24-month-forward EPS at December 2027 about 22 to 24 EUR (bear EPS and the trough band of 11x to 12x) about 33 EUR (the model's 33.94 less this set's EPS cut) about 38 to 40 EUR (bull EPS and a re-rating to 17x after a leverage print below 2.5x or a stake sale) -25% / +30%; skewed up only if the balance-sheet event arrives

Why the operating cone is skewed down (and why that is still a narrow cone):

  • The supply cap removes the right tail: Theon cannot ship more OCCAR units than Exosens makes tubes for, and new bilateral orders need 18 months. A beat can come only from thermal, platform and HGH lines, which are a third of revenue, so a 5% revenue beat is near the ceiling.
  • The left tail is open and has a base rate: about one in three comparable German call-offs has slipped a quarter or more since 2023, and a slip in Q4 (where 30% to 35% of annual sales fall) moves the full-year margin by 0.5 to 0.7 points on top of the revenue.
  • But the width is bounded by the contracted backlog: even the bear case is +14% revenue growth in FY2027, which is why the first set called revenue predictability high and I agree.

Why the balance-sheet cone is bimodal, and what that does to predictability:

  • The largest single swing in the 18 months is a management decision (sell the Exosens stake or not), not an operating outcome. The sale is tax-free in Cyprus, the stake is liquid (about 283 EURm), the strategic case for holding it weakened when the board-seat plan was dropped in September 2026, and both Deutsche Bank and Bernstein have pointed to it. Against: management bought it for supply coordination and has not said it will sell. I keep the second set's 30% inside 18 months.
  • A tighter covenant raises, not lowers, the probability of the bullish balance-sheet branch: if the June 2027 test looks close, the fastest fix is the stake, not equity, so the covenant tail converts mostly into de-risking. The exception is a correlated shock, when Exosens' own price would be down 20% to 30% in the same sell-off and the proceeds would be 200 to 230 EURm rather than 283 EURm; even then the sale would take leverage below 2.0x.
  • The reported-EPS line is wide for a reason unrelated to Theon: a 10% move in Exosens is ±0.36 EUR. Investors who use reported EPS will see FY2027 "growth" of anything from -20% to +25% against FY2026's 1.45 EUR; investors on the adjusted basis will see +21% in the base case.

Events that widen the cone, in order of their arrival:

  • The Q3 2026 trading update (9 November): orders and options rather than revenue; a book-to-bill below 1.0x for the nine months would widen the FY2027 revenue cone, not FY2026's.
  • The Bundestag committee session (12 November) and vote (27 November), and the Boxer decisions in December: the first evidence on whether the 2027 call-offs are on schedule.
  • A ceasefire or summit headline: the May to June 2026 precedent (sector -30%, Theon -4.8%) shows the multiple's sensitivity; from 2027 the operating sensitivity (option timing) is added.
  • Exosens' Q4 2026 and Q1 2027 reports: tube output and the Sturbridge transfer; any yield problem widens Theon's 2027 volume cone.
  • The HGH closing (late Q1 2027) and its purchase-price allocation: the amortisation and the presentation of adjusted EBIT.

Events that narrow it: the FY2026 results on 19 March 2027 (the finance-cost and tax split, the year-end net debt, the FY2027 guidance and the acquired-intangible treatment) resolve the three accounting ambiguities that account for about half of the FY2027 EPS width; a stake sale at any point collapses the balance-sheet cone to one branch.

Peer dispersion as a check on the width: MilDef's working capital fell ten points in a year (an upside the base case does not assume), Exosens' capacity ramp beat plan twice in 2026 (an upside to tube supply the base case does not assume), Hensoldt lost a 12.8 EURbn programme without warning in June 2026 (a tail the base case cannot price), and Exail's financing cost swung from 12% to 4% on one refinancing. For defence suppliers with one dominant framework and one supplier, outcomes have dispersed wider than for diversified peers in both directions; the base case here sits slightly below the middle of that range on cash and in the middle on operations.

So how predictable is the case: on a 60/20/20 weighting the FY2027 adjusted EPS cone is about 1.35 to 1.78 EUR around 1.63 (-17% / +9%), which is a medium-width cone with a downside skew; the end-2027 leverage cone is 1.1x to 3.0x, which is wide and bimodal; the revenue cone is ±8%, narrow for a company growing 24%. The investment case is predictable where it is least valuable and unpredictable where it is priced.

D-05

How helpful is the company's disclosure on operating momentum and key drivers of the thesis to monitor the case over about the next six months?

B+603rd of 30

Layer 3 · Risks

Judgement: helpful for the operating thesis and unhelpful for the financial thesis, and the next six months are the operating window. Theon discloses, quarterly, almost exactly the variables the revenue and mix case turns on, in the company's own definitions; it discloses nothing on interest, tax, free cash flow, the covenant, segment margins or OCCAR phasing, and its quarterly updates carry no cash-flow statement. The one rich data point in the window, the FY2026 results on 19 March 2027, arrives at the end of it, and the decisive print (H1 2027 leverage) falls outside it.

What arrives in the next six months, and which thesis variable each resolves:

Date Event What it discloses Which variable it settles What it cannot settle
9 November 2026 Q3 2026 trading update revenue (I expect about 132 EURm, +38%), revenue by product category and geography, order intake, soft backlog, options, gross margin, adjusted EBIT margin (about 23.5% on the company's basis), net working capital as a share of LTM revenue, cash conversion (EBITDA-based), net debt and leverage on the company's basis whether the nine-month book-to-bill is at or above 1.0x; whether non-night-vision stays above 20%; whether working capital is drifting above 41%; the H2 run-rate against the 351 EURm the guidance needs finance cost and tax (not reported quarterly); the OCCAR delivery schedule; cash flow by line
November to December 2026 contract announcements (the company announces awards routinely); the Bundestag session and vote; the Boxer decision; the 902 EURm of options "expected to be exercised shortly" the Q4 order season, which decides whether FY2027 to FY2028 revenue is firm or soft backlog option exercise and the pre-selected contracts the margin on any of it

19 March 2027 FY2026 results and annual report the full income statement (finance costs by line, tax, minorities), the cash-flow statement, year-end net debt and working capital, related-party balances, the Merio purchase-price allocation, FY2027 guidance (revenue, margin, acquired contribution, leverage), the adjusted-EBIT definition for 2027, the tax note by jurisdiction the three accounting ambiguities (finance, tax, amortisation); the FY2027 guidance against consensus; the H2 2026 working-capital outcome the June 2027 leverage test; OCCAR's 2027 phasing (only if the company chooses to give it)
May 2027 Q1 2027 trading update the first OCCAR-weighted quarter; HGH's first weeks whether the 2027 ramp has started on schedule outside the six months

What the disclosure does well:

  • The quarterly operating set is unusually complete for a company of this size: revenue by product category (Night, Thermal, Platform) lets the Consumer track the mix thesis every quarter; order intake, soft backlog and options separately let the book-to-bill and the option conversion be measured; the company reports working capital as a share of LTM revenue against a stated 35% target, and leverage against stated 2.5x and 3.5x thresholds.
  • Guidance is specific on the operating building blocks (revenue, margin floor, capex, mix targets, acquired contribution, synergies by 2029) and the record of raising mid-year gives the mid-year statements information content.
  • The acquisitions were announced with their revenue, margin, growth and backlog (HGH: about 40 EURm, above 40% EBITDA, 30% CAGR, 70 EURm backlog; Merio: above 15 EURm, above 3.5 EURm EBIT), which is more than most acquirers give.

What it does badly, and the consequence for monitoring:

  • No interest, tax, free-cash-flow or EPS guidance; no segment margins; no OCCAR phasing; no covenant definition; no disclosure of swap coverage or of the term loan's margin. The EPS-deciding lines are visible twice a year, in definitions that differ from FactSet's (net debt 237.9 EURm company against 213 EURm FactSet; adjusted EBIT 116.1 EURm against 104 EURm).
  • The quarterly "cash conversion" is EBITDA-based and ran at 83% while FactSet free cash flow was 17% of net income, so the one cash figure disclosed quarterly flatters.
  • The Q1 2026 cash-flow restatement (34.4 EURm of presentation error) and the restated FY2024 adjusted EBIT mean the semi-annual cash and profit figures need checking against the audited annual ones.
  • The Exosens marks make the reported quarterly EPS uninformative; the company does not publish an adjusted EPS.
  • Theon does not disclose tube deliveries, Harder's output or yields, or HGH's backlog phasing, so the supply ceiling has to be inferred from Exosens' reports.

External monitors that fill part of the gap inside the six months: Exosens' Q4 2026 release (tube volumes and Amplification revenue, of which Theon is about 40%); Hensoldt's Q4 order intake (German approvals and the joint venture); the Bundestag committee's published decisions; Exosens' share price (a daily read on Theon's reported EPS and on the value of the deleveraging option); and the loan-market press for any amendment to the facilities. Together with Theon's own quarterly set, these allow the operating case to be tracked almost in real time, and the balance-sheet case only at the two reporting dates.

The net: for the next six months the disclosure is adequate for what will happen (orders, mix, revenue) and poor for what will matter (cash and leverage); the Consumer should set the monitoring plan around the Q3 update, the Q4 order flow and the 19 March results, and expect no new information on the financial lines until then.

D-06

How clearly does what the company says it will achieve trace into specific operational drivers, onto identifiable revenue, cost and margin lines (the key fundamental milestones and measures), and through to earnings per share; and how clear is the external transmission from value-chain dynamics to earnings per share?

C509th of 30

Layer 3 · Risks

Judgement: the trace is clear from what the company promises to adjusted EBIT, and opaque from adjusted EBIT to earnings per share. The external transmission is clear for timing (budgets to call-offs to revenue) and for supply (Exosens' capacity to Theon's volume), partly clear for cost (tube price pass-through on bilateral work, fixed on OCCAR), and clear but unhelpful for reported earnings (Exosens' share price straight into the income statement). The weakest link in both chains is the same: the company has never said what interest, tax, amortisation and minorities will do to the earnings its operating promises generate.

The internal trace, promise by promise:

Capex about 30 EURm; payout 20% to 30% plants, Harder lines cash flow; dividend capex (disclosed); dividend declared high high for the dividend

What is missing from the trace, and what it costs: the company gives no guidance on net financial items (I use -18 to -27 EURm), tax (25%), minorities (1.5 to 5.5 EURm), acquired-intangible amortisation (about 10 to 11 EURm) or the Exosens marks (±28 EURm per 10% move). These five items move adjusted EPS by about 0.4 EUR a share between them in FY2027 against an EBIT-only reading, and reported EPS by more. An investor can derive each from the semi-annual accounts, but not from anything the company says it will achieve. That is why the first set found consensus on mixed bases for every year and why the FactSet revision pattern separates EBITDA from EPS.

The external transmission, dynamic by dynamic:

  1. German budget to call-offs to revenue (clear, dated, lagged): Einzelplan 14 up 29% in the 2027 draft; the 27 November vote; committee approvals above 25 EURm; then OCCAR and bilateral call-offs; then Theon's revenue 12 to 18 months later for new orders and within the year for scheduled tranches. Hensoldt's order intake and the committee's decisions are visible proxies; the one-in-three slip rate gives the error bar.
  2. Exosens' capacity to Theon's volume (clear in direction, opaque in numbers): Exosens' 40% capacity expansion by end-2027 was ahead of plan in 2026 and its FY2026 guidance was raised; OCCAR takes about 43% of its output in 2027 to 2029 and Theon's framework 52%. Exosens' quarterly Amplification revenue is the proxy; the annual breakdown of the 400,000 tubes is undisclosed.
  3. Tube price to gross margin (partly clear): bilateral and export work passes Exosens' 3% to 4% annual price rise through at a constant gross-margin percentage; OCCAR's tubes are fixed for ordered volumes with annual adjustment on placements, and OCCAR's end price is fixed with indexation capped near 10%. The exposed element is the non-tube 45% of OCCAR cost (German and Greek wages), which no disclosure tracks.
  4. Agency procurement to margin (clear in direction, slow): agency buying rising from about 20% to 40% of European procurement by 2030 lowers unit prices on the largest awards; it shows as a gross-margin drift of a few tenths of a point a year, inside the noise of mix.
  5. Euribor to interest to EPS (clear, unhedged in part): 100 basis points is about 6 EURm and 4% of EPS; swaps exist but their coverage is not disclosed.
  6. Exosens' share price to reported EPS (mechanical, untaxed): 10% is 0.36 EUR; the market can compute it daily, which is why the sell-side splits on whether to include it.
  7. Sector sentiment to the multiple (partly clear): the land-defence de-rating (EV/EBIT 21.6x to 17.2x, January to September 2026) reached Theon with a beta of 0.8 and an R-squared of about a quarter; the rest of the multiple is Theon's own leverage story (D-07).

The milestones that would show the trace working, in the order they can be observed: non-night-vision above 20% of revenue at each quarter; options exercised in Q4 2026; working capital at or below 41% at year-end; FY2026 margin at or above 26.4% on the company's basis; the FY2027 guidance stating the adjusted-EBIT definition; HGH closed by end-March 2027; June 2027 leverage at or below 2.9x; end-2027 below 2.5x. Each is identifiable and dated, which is the strength of this case; none of them is expressed by the company in earnings per share, which is its weakness.

The Investment Analyst's heading Perception and sentiment shocks: what sudden, high-magnitude risks exist, including events elsewhere that could hit the share price whatever the fundamentals?

Judgement: several live ones, amplified by a 30% float, about 7.8 USDm a day of liquidity, and a risk-off, fragility-penalising tape.

  • A Venetus placement.

C-61How strong is the company's record of acting, not just talking: a recent record of huge delivery and action, and a mid-term track record of delivery, not misses or justifications, whatever the environment (outperforming in tougher ones is even better), beyond its records against guidance and estimates?B767th of 30full answer under 4b Management quality
C-70

Beyond meeting or beating, what is the record, nature and behaviour of the company and its chief executive in upgrading guidance?

B+803rd of 30

Layer 1 · Guidance

The Investment Analyst's heading Upgrading behaviour: beyond meeting or beating, what is the record, nature and behaviour of the company and its chief executive in upgrading guidance?

Judgement: a consistent "under-promise, raise mid-year, over-deliver slightly" pattern. The raises are frequent and small, and they come from conservative bases. It is the sandbagging-precision archetype rather than step-change raising.

  • FY2024: guided at 330–350 EURm, delivered 352.4 EURm.
  • FY2025: guided at 410–430 EURm, raised mid-year to 435–445 EURm, delivered 443.4 EURm. The margin was guided at mid-twenties and delivered at 26.2%.
  • FY2026: guided at 570–600 EURm, then c. 600 EURm. The margin went from mid-twenties to above 26%.
  • Medium term: the 1 EURbn target was pulled forward a year, and the "bolt-on" qualifier was dropped.
  • The chief executive: "We have over-delivered on our promises so far."
  • The one gap: short-term profit guidance was withdrawn during the 2025 rights issue, for Cyprus regulatory reasons.
  • For Set 3: expect revenue to land at or slightly above the top of guidance, and the margin 0 to 50 basis points above the guided floor.
C-69

What is the company's record of missing, meeting or beating guidance and sell-side estimates over the last one, three and five years, including in comparable environmental conditions?

B706th of 30

Layer 1 · Sell-side estimates

The Investment Analyst's heading Delivery record: what is the record of missing, meeting or beating guidance and estimates over one, three and five years, including in comparable conditions?

Judgement: a consistent beat record on revenue and EBIT over the 2.6 listed years, with misses only on balance-sheet items. No five-year or downturn record exists.

  • FY2024: beat the top of revenue guidance.
  • FY2025: revenue beat consensus by 1%, EBIT by 7%, and Q4 EBIT by 11%.
  • Q1 2026:
  • Revenue -1.3% against consensus, but EBIT +3.9% and EBITDA +4.1%.
  • Orders beat Deutsche Bank by 15.5%.
  • Net debt was 18% worse than consensus.
  • H1 2026, against Deutsche Bank: revenue +3.1%, orders +4.3%, EBIT +9.2%, margin +150 basis points. Net debt was 8.8% worse.
  • Comparable conditions: all of this is in an upcycle. Pre-IPO growth averaged about 50% to 60% a year, but no estimate record exists for it.
  • For Set 3: a lean of about +1% to +3% on revenue and +3% to +7% on EBIT is supported by the record. Lean net debt the other way, worse than consensus.
P-06

After assessing the track record of delivery in greater detail, how much weight should the forecasts for the next 18 months to four years carry, and in which direction should they be leaned?

C+558th of 30

Layer 2 · Financial forecasts

Judgement: give high weight to the operating forecasts for FY2026 and FY2027 and lean them slightly up. Give medium weight to EPS for FY2026 and FY2027. Give low weight to cash and net debt, and lean them worse. Give medium-to-low weight to everything from FY2028, and lean revenue below consensus and the company's 2029 ambition.

  • The record (2.6 listed years) is an unbroken beat-and-raise on revenue and margin, and an unbroken miss on net debt.
  • It was built in a regime that is ending: demand-pull, short-cycle, rising prices, net cash, one country. The coming regime is fixed-price, supply-capped, leveraged and multi-country. That justifies smaller leans than the record alone would give.

1. The record, by line.

  • Revenue:
  • FY2024 delivered 352.4 EURm against guidance of 330 to 350 EURm.
  • FY2025 delivered 443.4 EURm against 410 to 430 EURm, raised to 435 to 445 EURm, and beat consensus by 1%.
  • Q1 2026 came in 1.3% below consensus. H1 2026 beat Deutsche Bank by 3.1%.
  • FY2026 guidance moved from 570–600 EURm to c. 600 EURm.
  • Margin and EBIT:
  • FY2025 margin 26.2% against "mid-twenties"; EBIT beat consensus by 7%.
  • H1 2026 EBIT beat Deutsche Bank by 9.2% (+150 basis points).
  • The FY2026 margin guide was raised to above 26%, and the medium-term target to above 26%.
  • Net debt and cash:
  • Q1 2026 net debt 18% worse than consensus; H1 2026 8.8% worse than Deutsche Bank.
  • The Q1 2026 cash-flow statement was restated (a 34.4 EURm presentation error).
  • FactSet free cash flow was negative in FY2024 and 17% of net income in FY2025.
  • Earnings quality:
  • The FY2024 adjusted EBIT was restated up by 2.45 EURm (joint-venture reclassification).
  • The FY2022 comparatives were restated for revenue recognition.
  • Brokers' FY2026 EPS spans 1.28 to 1.95 EUR on inconsistent treatment of the Exosens marks.

2. Weight and lean by line and horizon.

Line FY2026 FY2027 FY2028–FY2030

3. Why the leans are smaller than the record alone would justify.

  • The record's beats came from demand pulling forward into a short-cycle business with stock and tube priority. From 2027, OCCAR deliveries are scheduled and the tube is the constraint. The source of the revenue beats (selling more than the schedule) is mostly gone for night vision, and must come from non-night-vision.
  • Margin beats came from mix arriving faster than modelled (non-night-vision at 24% of Q2 2026 revenue against a 20% target for FY2026). That source continues, so a small margin lean up for FY2026 is justified. For FY2027 it is offset by OCCAR's first bulk year, which has no precedent in the record.
  • Execution of four integrations in four countries at once is untested, with a CFO from a small domestic group and a homogeneous team.
  • The balance-sheet record is consistently worse than forecast, and nothing in the coming regime improves it before 2028.

4. Direction of the lean in one sentence for Set 3: up a little on FY2026 and FY2027 revenue and FY2026 margin; flat on FY2027 margin; down on FY2028 and FY2029 revenue relative to consensus; worse on net debt and free cash flow through FY2028; and treat any FY2027 EPS within ±5% of 1.68 EUR as on-plan.

C-49What earnings-per-share growth is the company generating, with what revenue growth, and how durable is it?A801st of 30full answer under 4c Financial profile
Group judgement

Financial forecasts

A683rd of 30

Layer 2 · Financial forecasts

Written last by the Investment Analyst for its group

The financial forecasts are strong in level and modest in surprise, and the tube cap and the leverage tax on the lines below EBIT bound them in both directions.

  • Revenue: more than doubles in five years (443 to 1,027 EURm) and is contracted and supply-paced through FY2027.
  • Margin: holds at about 26.4%, double the defence median, after four years of decline.
  • EPS: compounds at about 18.5% a year to FY2028 and 11% after.
  • Free cash flow: for the first time, rises steadily, from about 30 EURm to about 135 EURm.

What limits the positive reading for an investment case:

  1. No margin expansion. Operating leverage is low and asymmetric, pricing is pass-through, and the mix gains are taken by OCCAR and localisation.
  2. Growth after FY2027 depends on non-night-vision lines that are a fifth to a third of revenue, carry no moat yet and are priced against primes. The company's 1 EURbn and 50%-non-night-vision 2029 ambition is about a year too early on my arithmetic.
  3. The EPS line pays for the strategy before it benefits. Interest peaks at about 25 EURm in FY2027, and reported EPS falls about 0.15 EUR a year below adjusted from FY2027, because of acquired-intangible amortisation, with the Exosens marks adding noise.
  4. The leverage target is met by a hair (2.44x against below 2.5x), and the record says lean net debt worse.

What supports it:

  1. FY2026 to FY2027 revenue and EBIT are predictable, with a mild upside lean from the beat-and-raise record.
  2. From FY2028, deleveraging is mechanical: slower growth absorbs less working capital. This is a reinforcing positive loop on EPS and cash that does not need management to do anything new.
  3. The Exosens stake is a 283 EURm option that would take leverage to about 1.1x and add about 4% to 5% to EPS.

For Set 3:

  • The concentration of drivers is high: tube allocation and OCCAR phasing, HGH's contribution, and working capital decide FY2027.
  • The cone is skewed to the downside on operations and to the upside only through a balance-sheet decision.

For Set 4: consensus is broadly right on operations, too light on interest, too high on free cash flow, and on mixed bases for EPS in every year:

  • FY2026: the Exosens marks;
  • FY2027 and FY2028: acquired-intangible amortisation;
  • FY2029: three brokers and an implied 89m shares.

The likely revision pattern over the next 18 months is the one already visible: operating lines up, EPS flat to slightly down, the gap closing only when leverage visibly falls.

P-08

After assessing below-operating-profit cost items (e.g. tax, interest, one-offs, minority investments, etc.) and share buybacks or issuance in greater detail, how is it likely to shape earnings per share, net income and free cash flow over the next 18 months to four years, versus the last four to six years?

D3822nd of 30

Layer 2 · Financial forecasts

Judgement: the lines below EBIT take about 4 points a year off EPS growth in FY2026 to FY2027 and then give back about 1 to 2 points a year over FY2028 to FY2030.

  • History: these lines were negligible (net interest 1 to 5 EURm until FY2024, no minorities). The share count was the main leak: +26% from FY2023 to FY2026 through the IPO, placings and the rights issue.
  • Forecast: interest is the main leak, peaking in FY2027. The share count is flat.
  • Two items make reported earnings persistently noisier than adjusted earnings:
  • the Exosens fair-value marks;
  • from 2027, acquired-intangible amortisation of about 10 to 11 EURm a year.
  • Free cash flow conversion recovers from 26% (FY2026) to about 70% of adjusted net income by FY2029–FY2030, because growth slows. It stays below consensus through FY2028.

2. Each item against its history, and what would move it.

  • Interest: from -1.1 to -4.9 EURm (FactSet, FY2021 to FY2024) and -9.5 EURm (FY2025, FactSet; -6.8 EURm net on the company's basis) to a peak of -25 EURm (FY2027).

  • HGH (France, 25%) and Germany (about 30%) push the rate up. Not a swing factor: ±1 point is ±0.02 EUR.
  • Minorities: from 0.74 EURm (FY2025) to about 5.5 EURm (FY2030): Harder 34.78%, Merio 20%, the Safran joint venture 49% and the Twin Prime joint venture 40%. That is about 0.07 EUR a share by FY2030, small but rising.
  • Associate and joint-venture income: the Hensoldt–Theon joint venture is inside operating profit since FY2025 (+80 basis points on margin, Deutsche Bank). It is the cash at risk in the joint venture's receivables, not a below-EBIT item.
  • Non-recurring items: 7.3 EURm (FY2025) and 3.89 EURm in H1 2026 (against 0.45 EURm in H1 2025). I model about 8.5, 4 and 2 EURm. A recurring "non-recurring" line is itself a signal. If M&A continues, brokers should stop adding it back.
  • Acquired-intangible amortisation:
  • Kappa recognised 27.1 EURm of intangibles and 37.6 EURm of goodwill; in my reading its amortisation is already inside the company's adjusted EBIT.
  • HGH (about 300 EURm enterprise value) is my estimate: about 90 to 100 EURm of intangibles over 9 to 10 years, about 10 EURm a year. Merio adds about 1.2 EURm a year.
  • Hensoldt's precedent: 45 EURm of purchase-price effects in FY2025 between adjusted EBITDA and EBIT.
  • The Set 4 point: consensus D&A jumps from 10.7 EURm (FY2027) to 15.8 EURm (FY2028), which suggests that some brokers already deduct HGH amortisation and others do not. So FY2027 to FY2028 consensus EPS is also on mixed bases.
  • Share count:
  • 62.4m (FY2023), 72.8m, 77.6m basic and 78.6m diluted after the December 2025 rights issue (8.6m shares at 17.40 EUR).
  • The incentive-plan buyback held 1,033,096 treasury shares in June 2026, out of an authority for 1.4m.
  • I model about 3 to 5 EURm a year of incentive-plan purchases, offset by vesting (+0.3m to +0.4m shares a year from FY2027), so the count is roughly flat at 77.6m to 78.2m.
  • No buyback for return before leverage is below about 2x (FY2028 at the earliest). The downside branch is an equity raise (about 10% inside 18 months) of 5m to 8m shares: -6% to -9% on EPS.
  • Dividends: payout cut to 20% to 30% (November 2025). I model 0.32, 0.37, 0.45, 0.53 and 0.60 EUR for FY2026 to FY2030, declared for the year and paid the following June. That is 22% to 25% of adjusted EPS, against consensus of 0.32 / 0.36 / 0.46 / 0.53 EUR.

4. Against the last four to six years.

  • Adjusted EPS grew about 42% a year over FY2023 to FY2025 (0.577 to 1.16 EUR) with negligible below-EBIT leakage but heavy share issuance.
  • It grows about 18.5% a year over FY2025 to FY2028 with the opposite mix: flat shares and heavy interest.
  • Then it grows about 11% a year to FY2030, with the below-EBIT lines a tailwind.
  • Net income quality moves the other way to cash quality: reported earnings get noisier (marks, amortisation, non-recurring items), while cash conversion improves from FY2028.

4. The cone over the next 18 months (FY2027 is the year that decides it).

  • Base: revenue 752 EURm, adjusted EPS 1.68 EUR, end-2027 leverage 2.44x.
  • Bear, about 20% probability:
  • What happens: an OCCAR tranche or option call-off slips (budget approvals; a ceasefire; Exosens yield trouble), revenue falls about 8%, the margin is 100 basis points lower because the fixed block is committed, and rates are 100 basis points higher.
  • Result: revenue about 690 EURm, EBIT about 176 EURm, EPS about 1.40 EUR (-17%), leverage about 2.75x.
  • In the tail (about 10%), add a discounted equity raise of 5m to 8m shares (EPS -6% to -9% more).
  • Bull, about 20% probability: non-night-vision converts faster (as in 2026), the margin is 50 basis points higher and the Exosens stake is sold. EPS about 1.80 to 1.85 EUR (+7% to +10%), leverage about 1.1x.
  • So the cone for FY2027 adjusted EPS is about 1.40 to 1.85 EUR, skewed to the downside on operations, because volume upside is capped and downside is not. The only large upside is a balance-sheet decision.
  • FY2026 is narrow: adjusted EPS 1.33 to 1.45 EUR. The width is the H1 finance-cost ambiguity and Q4 delivery timing.

5. The checkpoints that would move this base case.

  • Q3 2026 trading update, 9 November 2026: revenue about 132 EURm (+38%), margin about 23% to 24%, book-to-bill and option exercise.
  • FY2026 results, 19 March 2027: revenue about 605 EURm, margin about 26.4%, net financial items (the ambiguity resolved), net debt about 250 EURm, FY2027 guidance (I expect organic growth above 15% plus 55 to 60 EURm acquired), and how the company presents acquired-intangible amortisation.
  • HGH closing, Q1 2027: the price paid in cash, and the purchase-price allocation.
  • H1 2027 report, September 2027: leverage at or below about 2.9x at the seasonal peak, the first HGH half, the OCCAR ramp's gross margin.
  • FY2027 results, March 2028: leverage below 2.5x, or not.

The Investment Analyst's heading Scope to improve outputs: beyond cost cuts, how much plausible scope is there over the mid term, do peer precedents show it is achievable, does it come from a few big areas or many small, and is it congruent with strategy?

C-80

To what extent has the core growth engine decelerated over the last twelve months: is the revenue growth rate running below the company's own normal rate, and is the input change force itself slowing?

A823rd of 30

Layer 1 · Risks

The Investment Analyst's heading Growth deceleration: how much has the core growth engine decelerated over the last twelve months? Is revenue growth below the company's normal rate, and is the input force itself slowing?

Judgement: there is no deceleration over the last twelve months. Both the output and the input have accelerated. The deceleration is ahead, in consensus, and is mostly mechanical: the tube cap and the larger base.

  • Output: revenue growth of +35.4% in H1 2026 (24.7% organic) against +25.8% in FY2025. H2 2026 is guided to about +35%.
  • Input: order intake +38.5% in H1, Q2 a record for a non-Q4 quarter, and the backlog up 2.5% quarter on quarter.
  • Ahead: consensus organic growth slows to about 15% to 17% in FY2027 and about 14% to 17% in FY2028.
  • Watch: the book-to-bill at the 9 November 2026 Q3 update and at FY2026. A reading below 1.0x would show the input slowing.

Layer 1 · Sell-side estimates

The Investment Analyst's heading Forecasts against history: how do the sell-side's forecasts for the next one to three years compare with the last one to five, for the main lines and the granular build, and how credible is the gap?

Judgement: the revenue and EBIT forecasts are a credible deceleration from history. The EBITDA margin expansion is credible only with HGH and mix. The free-cash-flow step-up is the least credible line, because it needs working capital to improve in a way the history does not show. EPS is distorted by inconsistent bases.

Line by line, history against consensus (FactSet; the brokers' basis where stated):

  • Revenue: +53% / +61% / +26% (FY2023–FY2025), then +35% / +24% / +17% / +14%. Credible: the backlog covers it, and acquisitions add about 55 to 60 EURm in 2027. The required non-NVG growth is the swing factor.
  • Gross margin: 32.1% / 31.0% / 32.6%, then 34.5% / 33.8% / 34.3% (gross profit 207 / 251 / 298 EURm on four brokers). Credible after H1 2026's 35.1%, but it ignores the OCCAR mix risk in 2027–2028.
  • EBITDA margin (brokers' basis): 25.2% (FY2025), then 27.2% / 28.0% / 28.4% / 26.4%. HGH (above 40%) and the D&A step-up explain most of the expansion.

  • Net interest: 9.5 EURm (FY2025). Implied by EBIT less pre-tax profit, about 3 / 20 / 16 / 26 EURm. The FY2026 figure is distorted by fair-value or associate income; FY2027 at about 20 EURm is plausible to slightly light for about 500 to 550 EURm of average net debt.
  • Tax and minorities: implied about 22% to 27% of pre-tax profit, inconsistent across brokers.
  • Shares: implied about 79m to 81m against 78.6m diluted; FY2029 implies 89m, an artefact.
  • Capex: 2.6% of revenue (FY2025), then 5.2% / 4.5% / 4.3%. Credible.
  • Free cash flow: 13.6 EURm (FY2025), then 39 / 85 / 115 / 105 EURm. This needs incremental working capital at about 15% to 20% of new revenue against a stock ratio of 41%. It is optimistic unless prepayments or a working-capital programme appear.
  • Dividends per share: 0.31 EUR, then 0.32 / 0.36 / 0.46 / 0.53. Credible.

P-09

After assessing the other questions/items in the set in greater detail, what is the net likely impact to the main financial line items over the next 18 months to four years, versus the last four to six years?

B+683rd of 30

Layer 2 · Financial forecasts

Judgement: Theon moves from one shape to another.

  • Old shape: hyper-growth, falling margin, net cash, heavy share issuance. Revenue grew 52% a year over FY2020 to FY2025, the FactSet EBIT margin fell from 31.6% to 23.4%, and EPS grew about 42% a year over FY2023 to FY2025.
  • New shape: mid-teens growth, flat margin, leverage first and deleveraging after.
  • Revenue grows about 24% a year over FY2025 to FY2028 and 18% to FY2030.
  • The adjusted margin is flat at about 26.4%.
  • Adjusted EPS grows about 18.5% a year to FY2028 and about 11% a year after.
  • The net is a slower but cleaner compounding of operating profit. For two years it is taxed by interest; after that, deleveraging and rising cash conversion help it.
  • The most important change is cash. Free cash flow goes from erratic and negative (-42 EURm in FY2024) to steadily rising (about 90 EURm in FY2028, 135 EURm in FY2030). The history never showed that, and FY2028 is the first year it can be tested.

The lines, history against forecast.

How the composition of EPS growth changes.

  • FY2023 to FY2025: revenue +42% a year, less margin dilution, less share issuance. Negligible interest.
  • FY2025 to FY2028: revenue +24.4% a year, no margin change, below-EBIT lines about -3% a year, share count about -3.5% a year (the rights issue landing in FY2026). EPS +18.5%.
  • FY2028 to FY2030: revenue +9.7% a year, margin about -0.1 points, below-EBIT lines about +1.5% a year, shares flat. EPS +11.3%.

The judgements that follow.

  1. Growth slows by half and then by half again, by construction. The FY2026 growth (+36%) is the last year in which acquired revenue (Kappa) and an unconstrained night-vision line combine. From FY2027, the tube cap and the scheduled OCCAR profile leave non-night-vision as the only accelerator, and it is a fifth to a third of revenue.
  2. The margin stops falling, which is the improvement over history, but it does not rise. For a business that lost eight points of margin in four years, a flat 26.4% is a better outcome than the past. It is also below what a "mix shift to software and thermal" story would suggest, because OCCAR and localisation take the gain.
  3. EBIT growth now equals revenue growth, where historically it was below it. That is the one place where the next four years are better than the last four.
  4. Earnings quality diverges by measure. Reported EPS is noisier than adjusted EPS (marks, amortisation, recurring "non-recurring" items): the gap is about 0.15 EUR a year from FY2027. Cash quality improves from FY2028. An investor who uses reported EPS will see a weaker profile in FY2027 (1.53 EUR, +5% on FY2026 reported) than one who uses adjusted EPS (+21%).
  5. The return on capital falls by half and recovers only slowly. This is the cost of buying the strategy (Exosens 268.7 EURm, Kappa, HGH about 300 EURm, Merio) rather than building it. It justifies a lower multiple than the organic business alone would earn, until the acquired assets show their returns.

Memo topic 7, Scope for upgrades over 6 to 12 months: the Rater's score

Behind the rating as a whole

Group judgement

The fourth set as a whole

C-4413th of 30

Layer 4 · The fourth set as a whole

Written last by the Investment Analyst for its group

The gap between our assessment and what guidance, consensus and the narrative already reflect is small on the operating lines, negative and partly anticipated on earnings per share, negative and unanticipated on free cash flow, and favourable only on timing: the market knows what it is worried about (leverage) and is waiting for the print that answers it, which falls ten to eleven months away.

  • Guidance is conservative where I can test it (revenue and margin for FY2026 and FY2027 sit at or just below my base, with a record of mid-year raises) and silent where I disagree (interest, tax, cash). The medium-term targets are where it is ahead of me: the 1 EURbn by 2029, the 50% mix and the above-15% organic growth all meet the tube cap in my numbers, and the above-26% margin faces an optical miss from amortisation. Favourable for the next two events, unfavourable for the FY2028 guidance.
  • Consensus is at my numbers on EBIT for FY2027, 3% above on FY2028 EBIT, 2% to 6% above on EPS and 18% to 26% above on free cash flow to FY2028, and on mixed bases in every year. The revision pattern already points the way I lean (EBITDA up, EPS down), so the direction of my disagreement is priced and the magnitude is not; the first cut (the Exosens reversal) is mechanical and near; the free-cash-flow line is the one with no revision process and the most room to disappoint.
  • The narrative articulates the leverage peak, the interest drag, the HGH price and the stake as a buffer, and it does not articulate the tube cap in the out-year numbers, the amortisation inside adjusted EBIT, the tax rate, the covenant definitions or the governance items. Investor commentary is unobserved. Of the unarticulated items, the negatives are small and the two positives (deleveraging becoming mechanical from FY2028; the stake's tax-free liquidity) are the ones a memo can add.

What this means for the investment case: the market has already marked the stock to the balance-sheet question and is not paying for the operating delivery that is the predictable half of the case. The gap closes favourably if, and when, the balance-sheet question closes: a June 2027 leverage print at or below 2.9x followed by an end-2027 print below 2.5x, or a stake sale at any time, would re-rate the stock toward Theon's own two-year median multiple of 17x on earnings that consensus already carries, which is the 15% to 20% the first set sized plus the earnings growth. It closes unfavourably if the next two net-debt prints repeat the pattern of the last two. Before the H1 2027 report the calendar offers operating catalysts the market has said it will not pay for and one accounting-driven EPS cut, so the near-term gap is neutral at best; over 18 months it is modestly favourable, conditional on an event whose probability I put at about 65% (the target met narrowly, or the stake sold).
