Appendix Research requests the Investment Analyst raised for Layer 3 and Layer 4

Research requests for the next set

Company: Theon International Plc (THEON-NL, Euronext Amsterdam): a Greek-founded, founder-controlled maker of man-portable night-vision and thermal imaging systems for armed forces (night vision was 93% of FY2025 revenue), expanding into platform-based optronics (Kappa Optronics; the PHYLAX system for Rheinmetall), drone gimbals (Merio; a 51%-owned joint venture with Safran), multi-domain surveillance and counter-drone systems (HGH Systèmes Infrarouges, closing early 2027) and image intensifier tubes (65.22% of Harder Digital), and holding a 9.8% stake in its main tube supplier, Exosens; it reports revenue by product category (night vision, thermal, platform-based, other) and by geography, with no segment margins.

Request: T-D1

Peers: Exosens (EXENS-FR; the tube supplier whose capacity governs Theon's night-vision volume, and a new downstream competitor through NightVision Lasers Spain and its US Army BiNOD contract); Hensoldt (HAG-DE; Theon's OCCAR joint-venture partner, Optronics margin ramp, customer prepayments); MilDef (MILDEF-SE; framework-based assembler that cut net working capital to 31.2% of sales and net debt to 0.7x EBITDA by Q3 2026); Exail Technologies (EXA-FR; leveraged post-merger integration and refinancing of the iXblue deal); Elbit Systems (vertically integrated night vision after buying Harris Night Vision in 2019; indexed contracts); L3Harris (BiNOD supplier; the LHX NeXt cost programme); Leonardo DRS (sensing segment margins and deleveraging); Safran Electronics & Defense and Thales (optronics divisions of the European primes and platform competitors); Teledyne FLIR (thermal imaging); private: Photonis Defense (Exosens' US arm), Senop, NightVision Lasers Spain. Operating conditions: executing one very large fixed-price multinational framework (OCCAR) while integrating four acquisitions in four countries; a supply-constrained critical component (image intensifier tubes with about 18-month lead times; gallium refined mostly in China); net leverage near 3x EBITDA during integration; a sector de-rating with rising bond yields; a ceasefire or slower procurement after 2027; the move from analog to fused and digital goggles; local-content rules in Europe (SAFE, EDIP) and the Gulf. Inputs: How many tubes a year can Theon obtain from Exosens under the agreement for over 400,000 tubes over 2026–2030 (announced 3 December 2025) and from Harder Digital (about 15,000 tubes of capacity in 2025 rising to 20,000 in 2027, company), and how much of that the OCCAR programme consumes (100,000 binoculars, about 200,000 tubes, deliveries 2027–2029, Bernstein 10 December 2025)? Can an assembler that buys its main component from one supplier grow night-vision revenue faster than its tube supply, and what precedents exist? How plausible is Theon's target of non-night-vision revenue at 50% by 2029 and EUR 1bn of group revenue by 2029 (November 2025 Capital Markets Day, pulled forward in 2026), given the scale of IRIS-C thermal clip-ons, Kappa and PHYLAX, Harder third-party tubes, Merio, the Safran gimbal joint venture and HGH today? Which companies have moved from a single core product to about half of revenue elsewhere within four years largely by acquisition, how many succeeded, how long it took, and what distinguished success? Can Theon hold an adjusted EBIT margin above 26% (company target) while moving work to Belgium, Germany, France and the Gulf; what did Hensoldt–ESG, Exosens–NVLS and Exail–iXblue show? Has any defence assembler cut net working capital from about 40% of revenue (Theon, H1 2026) toward 30% to 35% while growing above 15% a year, and how? How often have companies deleveraged from about 3x to below 2.5x within about twelve months of a debt-funded acquisition made at the top of an order cycle? Focus: Whether Theon's demonstrated strengths (low-cost Greek assembly, priority tube allocation, qualification lock-in, sales execution) are enough for the change now expected, or whether the plan relies on strengths it has not yet shown: multi-country integration, working-capital conversion, and building non-night-vision lines to about half of revenue against established primes.

Request: T-D2

Peers: Exosens (EXENS-FR; tube capacity, yields and allocation are a shared driver); Hensoldt (HAG-DE; OCCAR partner, optronics capacity, a cancelled-programme precedent with F126); MilDef (MILDEF-SE; framework call-off timing and working-capital swings); Exail Technologies (EXA-FR; leverage and financing-cost dispersion); Kongsberg (KOG-NO; a defence electronics name whose outcomes were concentrated on a few programmes); Rheinmetall (Theon's PHYLAX customer; German approval timing); Elbit Systems and L3Harris (US night-vision programmes); private: Photonis Defense, NightVision Lasers Spain. Operating conditions: a year in which one framework (OCCAR, through the Hensoldt–Theon joint venture) is about 38% of revenue (one European customer was 38% of FY2025 revenue); German purchases above EUR 25m awaiting the Bundestag budget committee (vote on 27 November 2026); possible ceasefire headlines; floating-rate debt; the HGH closing and its cash outflow in Q1 2027; strongly fourth-quarter-weighted deliveries; Exosens' fair-value marks flowing through reported earnings. Inputs: Which few drivers separate Theon's next 18 months to four years from its last four to six years: tube allocation and OCCAR delivery phasing; the OCCAR margin against bilateral contracts (Deutsche Bank cut EBIT 3% on it, January 2026); the HGH contribution (about EUR 40m of revenue at an EBITDA margin above 40%, company and Bernstein June 2026); the speed of non-night-vision growth; working capital (about 41% of revenue at June 2026); the interest bill on the EUR 300m revolving facility (Euribor plus 1.5%) and the EUR 325m term loan (7 September 2026); the accounting for acquired-intangible amortisation; the Exosens share price? How wide is the dispersion of broker forecasts (FactSet, 28 October 2026: FY2026 EPS from EUR 1.28 at Deutsche Bank to EUR 1.95 at Marex; FY2027 revenue from EUR 712m at Oddo BHF to EUR 780m at UBS), and how much is basis rather than view? What was the historical dispersion of outcomes for defence companies after an order boom, which events widened or narrowed it (ceasefires, approval delays, supplier yield problems, export-licence refusals, covenant pressure), and how often were forecasts resting on one or two programmes met? How does fourth-quarter concentration (about 30% to 35% of annual sales in November and December, company) affect the width of annual outcomes? Focus: How concentrated the 18-month outcome is on two or three drivers (OCCAR delivery timing within the tube supply, the HGH closing and the leverage path, and working-capital conversion), and how skewed the range is between a timing slip and faster conversion of the newer product lines.

Request: T-I1

Peers: Exosens (EXENS-FR; raised FY2026 guidance on a faster capacity ramp, 17 September 2026; its third-quarter gross-margin dip); Hensoldt (HAG-DE; free-cash-flow guidance record and prepayments); MilDef (MILDEF-SE; guidance on frame call-offs); Exail Technologies (EXA-FR; guidance during a takeover); Kongsberg (KOG-NO; results 29 October 2026); Rheinmetall (results 5 November 2026; German approvals); Renk (working-capital targets); private: Photonis Defense. Operating conditions: the Q3 2026 trading update (9 November 2026), the FY2026 results with FY2027 guidance (19 March 2027), the Q1 2027 update, the H1 2027 report (September 2027), the Q3 2027 update and the FY2027 results (March 2028); a sector in which estimates held while multiples fell from July to October 2026; rising rates; possible ceasefire headlines; the HGH closing in early 2027. Inputs: How likely is Theon to deliver FY2026 revenue above c. EUR 600m and an adjusted EBIT margin above 26% (guidance raised at H1 2026), given its record (FY2024 EUR 352.4m against EUR 330–350m guidance; FY2025 EUR 443.4m against EUR 410–430m, raised to EUR 435–445m)? What will FY2027 guidance look like in March 2027 (organic growth above 15%; management has said acquisitions add EUR 55–60m of 2027 sales), and will the company begin to guide interest, free cash flow or leverage? What did the 12-month revision pattern (FactSet to 31 October 2026: FY2027 EBITDA +16%, revenue +11%, EPS -3%) imply, and is it likely to persist? What would each outcome (raise, meet, cut) at each event need to happen, including option exercise (EUR 902m of contractual options at June 2026) and the pre-selected higher-value contracts announced on 30 September 2026? Focus: Whether the March 2027 guidance and the H1 2027 leverage print move consensus EPS up or down, given that operating estimates are rising while EPS estimates are flat to falling, and whether guidance on margin and leverage is likely to be met, beaten or missed over the next 18 months.

For each: which analysts (Deutsche Bank, ING, UBS, Stifel, Marex, Oddo BHF, Wood & Company, Bernstein on Exosens) and which investor commentators discuss it, how prominently, since when and with what view; which are widely discussed, which by one or two, and which by no one. Layer 3 and Layer 4: Assessing the predictability of the financial forecasts for the next 18 months / Assessing the extent to which our assessment of the foundational components and financial forecasts is reflected or not in current company guidance, sell-side estimates and stock market narrative, primarily for the next 18 months and secondarily for 18 months to 3 years out

Written 31 October 2026, 12:11 to 12:33 UK, Fable, main login

What the Investment Analyst was asked

The objective is to produce a robustly tested assessment of the predictability of the financial forecasts for the next 18 months, based on the first and second sets' answers.

The objective is to produce that judgement, based on the first and second sets' answers and your answers to the third set, for the Investment Analyst who writes the investment memo, so that Richard, who reads the memo, can form his judgement on the investment case.

What the scores mean in this layer

Finish each answer and each judgement on a group or set with a score from 1 to 100; a higher number is more positive for an investment case in the company.

For the third set's questions, the score says how positive the thing the question asks about is for the predictability of the company's financial forecasts over the next 18 months, and so for an investment case in the company: 100 the most positive, 1 the most negative, 50 neither.

For the fourth set's questions, the score says how favourable for an investment case in the company the gap is between our assessment and what current guidance, sell-side estimates and the stock market narrative already reflect: 100 the most favourable, 1 the least, 50 neither.

The Investment Analyst's title
Theon International (THEON-NL): third and fourth sets of questions, the predictability of the forecasts and what guidance, consensus and the narrative already reflect

As at 2026-10-31. Price 30.42 EUR (2026-10-30). Written for the Analyst who writes the investment memo.

The Investment Analyst's opening notes

Reading notes for the Consumer (read before the answers)

What is new since the second set, and what it changes

The AlphaSense reports commissioned by the second set, and the three template reports, add eight facts that matter for predictability. Each is dated and sourced in the reports; I give only what the Consumer needs.

  1. The H1 2026 finance-cost and tax ambiguity is resolved, and the answer is worse for tax than for interest. The interim report breaks net financial items down: interest expense 5.88 EURm (5.51 EURm on long-term loans), other finance expenses 3.73 EURm (letters of guarantee, fees), a net foreign-exchange gain of 0.93 EURm, bank interest income 0.17 EURm and fair-value gains of 37.24 EURm. Net financial items excluding the marks were therefore about -8.5 EURm, between the two readings the second set weighed and close to its -10 EURm midpoint. The fair-value gain was not taxed: the parent is Cyprus-resident, gains on listed securities are exempt, and the only deferred-tax item was a 0.32 EURm credit. That confirms the first set's clean H1 net income of about 37 EURm (74.35 less 37.24). But it also means the whole tax charge fell on operating profit: on reported EBIT of 61.25 EURm less about 8.5 EURm of finance cost, the 15 to 18 EURm of tax implied by the reported figures is an underlying rate of roughly 28% to 33%, not the 19.9% headline and not the 23% to 24% the company guides. Interim tax charges are estimates and this one may be trued down at the year-end; but HGH (France, about 25%) and the German units (about 30%) are growing, so I raise the tax rate to 25.0% in every forecast year (section 6). Each point of tax is about 0.02 EUR a share.

  2. Exosens pays Theon no dividend. The H1 2026 report shows no dividend income from the 9.8% stake, and Exosens reinvests to fund its capacity programme. The second set netted 3 to 5 EURm a year of Exosens dividends against interest. With guarantee and fee costs running at about 7 EURm a year (3.73 EURm in H1 alone) rather than the 4 EURm assumed, net financial items are 2 to 5 EURm a year heavier than the second set's from FY2027. I change them to -18, -27, -27, -24 and -21 EURm (section 6). FY2026 stays at -18 because the H1 actual was lighter than the midpoint.

  3. The covenant level is less settled than the earlier sets assumed. ING (after the H1 2026 results) and the first set's change-forces report put the bank covenant at 3.5x; the company's information memorandum of 1 December 2025 describes the 300 EURm revolving facility's covenants as net leverage of 3.00:1 and interest cover of 4.00:1. The 325 EURm term loan of 7 September 2026 is on "improved terms", management guided in June 2026 to a pro-forma peak of about 3.0x and said in September 2026 that it foresees no compliance difficulty; both point to the September package resetting the covenant to 3.5x, or to acquired EBITDA counting pro forma. I keep 3.5x as the working assumption, but the possibility that the revolving facility still carries 3.0x shrinks the cushion from about 16% of EBITDA to about 2% at the mid-2027 peak (net debt about 578 EURm against pro-forma LTM EBITDA of about 197 EURm, 2.9x). Two further points are now firmer: the annual report's definition of net debt (Note 39) nets cash and deposits only, so the Exosens stake cannot be netted for the covenant, contrary to Bernstein's 1.5x "underlying" leverage; and interest-rate swaps exist but their coverage is undisclosed, so the company's own 100-basis-point sensitivity (0.9 EURm) is stale and the second set's 5.5 EURm stands.

  4. The OCCAR commercial terms are partly resolved. Theon recognises its manufacturing share of OCCAR directly as product sales: the Hensoldt-Theon GbR is an invoicing agent (130.7 EURm of sales of goods to it in 2025), so there is no consolidation question. Prices are fixed and firm with annual adjustments capped at about 10% (former head of procurement). Exosens' framework prices are fixed at contract entry without automatic review clauses, so the second set's "tube price locked at about 2,500 EUR" is better read as fixed for ordered volumes and renegotiable on annual placements, with Theon's 40% share of Exosens' revenue as the restraint. No prepayments are visible: customer advance guarantees were 1.50 EURm and contract liabilities 3.69 EURm at June 2026; armed-forces terms are 0 to 120 days; related-party balances with the joint venture are unsecured and settled within six months. Receivables from the joint venture rose to 22.74 EURm at June 2026 from 6.55 EURm at end-2025 (the first set's 34.95 EURm at end-2025 is a different related-party line; the direction is what matters).

  5. The acquired-intangible amortisation sits inside the company's adjusted EBIT. Consolidated amortisation rose from 0.10 EURm (H1 2025) to 1.86 EURm (H1 2026) "driven entirely by Kappa", and the H1 bridge from reported to adjusted EBIT is exactly the non-recurring items. The second set's warning is confirmed: unless the company redefines adjusted EBIT when HGH closes (Exosens excludes purchase-price amortisation from adjusted profit, so a redefinition is plausible), its headline FY2027 adjusted margin would be about 25.1% against the "above 26%" target, with the business unchanged. Kappa's allocation (27.1 EURm of intangibles, 39% of the 69.9 EURm price) is the precedent for HGH.

  6. HGH closes in late Q1 2027, per the interim report, with a 10 EURm prepayment already made. The second set consolidates it from about February (42 EURm of FY2027 revenue). A late-March close gives about 37 EURm; the 5 EURm of revenue and about 1.7 EURm of EBIT lost are offset by about 1.5 EURm less interest, so I leave the inputs. Deutsche Bank's 18x pre-synergy EV/EBITDA implies HGH EBITDA of about 16.7 EURm on 40 EURm of revenue, consistent with the stated margin above 40%.

  7. German procurement timing now has a base rate. Contracts above 25 EURm need Bundestag budget-committee approval; comparable German programmes since 2023 have slipped by a quarter or more in about one third of cases (a cumulative 12.5 EURbn underspend over 2022 to 2025; truck call-offs delayed by the 2025 government formation; F126 cancelled in June 2026), against a record 103 approvals in 2025 and the Procurement Acceleration Act of January 2026. The 2027 draft core budget rises 29% to 109.7 EURbn with the equipment cash envelope up 21% to 63.4 EURbn; the committee's final session is 12 November and the vote 27 November 2026.

  8. Market and consensus data. Price 30.42 EUR; P/E 14.7x at the 39th percentile of ten years; technical momentum F, sell-side momentum C, valuation B; three-month return -12.6%, twelve-month +5.0%; relative-strength percentile 32. FY2027 consensus: revenue 743 EURm, EBITDA 203 to 208 EURm, EPS 1.65 to 1.66 EUR; revisions over twelve months EBITDA +16%, sales +11%, EPS -3%; seven brokers, all Buy; target 39.9 EUR. The peer basket explains 24% of Theon's daily variance over the last six months (beta 0.80). The model's return at the guideline 14.8x multiple is +16.2% to December 2027 and +33.4% to June 2029 before this set's input changes, which take about 3 points off each.

The model's cross-check gaps, explained

The one-page summary shows the Analyst's EPS below the model's by 0.03 to 0.07 EUR in FY2027 to FY2030 (1.68 against 1.71, 1.93 against 1.97, 2.19 against 2.25, 2.39 against 2.46) and net income below by 2.4 to 5.5 EURm. The whole gap is minority interests, which the model form does not hold (the summary states "Not modelled: minority interests"): the Analyst deducted 2.5, 3.5, 4.5 and 5.5 EURm for Harder (34.78%), Merio (20%), the Safran joint venture (49%) and Twin Prime (40%). In FY2026 the gap is 0.5 EURm against 1.5 EURm of minorities; the residual 1.0 EURm is the model's own rounding or tax arithmetic and is immaterial. No input is wrong on this account. The Consumer should take EPS after minorities (the cross-check, updated below) as the figure of record and read the model's EPS as before minorities.

The summary also notes that the FY2026 quarterly margins in Part 3 were moved up by 1.7 points to match the annual 26.4%. The cause is a basis difference, not an input error. The model's H1 2026 history is FactSet's EBIT (27.8 and 31.5 EURm, 23.8% of revenue, after non-recurring items), while Part 3's margins and Part 1's year are on the company's adjusted basis (65.14 EURm, 26.2%). The 2.4-point gap on the 41% of the year's revenue earned in H1 is 1.0 point on the year, which the model recovered by adding 1.7 points to the 59% of revenue in H2. The year is right; the model's Q3 and Q4 2026 margins (about 25.2% and 30.0%) overstate the Analyst's (23.5% and 28.3%) by exactly that artefact. The Financial Analyst should put the H1 2026 history on the company's adjusted-EBIT basis (65.14 EURm, 26.2%), or, if the model must keep FactSet's history, record that the H2 2026 phasing carries a 1.7-point basis offset, so that the Q3 print on 9 November 2026 (I expect about 23.5% on the company's basis) is not read as a miss against 25.2%.

The base case after this set's two input changes (section 6)

Two inputs change: the tax rate to 25.0% in every year, and net financial items to -18, -27, -27, -24 and -21 EURm. Revenue, margin, dividend, share-count and cash-flow inputs are unchanged.

  • Net debt is higher than the second set's by about 3 EURm (end-2026), 7 (end-2027), 13 (end-2028), 19 (end-2029) and 26 EURm (end-2030) from the extra interest and tax. End-2027 leverage is about 2.48x against the company's "below 2.5x in 2027": the same knife-edge, a hair closer.
  • Reported FY2026 EPS is about 1.45 EUR: the marks are untaxed, so the full-year gain of about 14 EURm (305.9 EURm at June, about 283 EURm at 56.9 EUR on 30 October) is 0.18 EUR, and each 10% move in Exosens' share price is about ±0.36 EUR of reported EPS, not the ±0.30 EUR the second set assumed after tax.
  • The model's 18-month return falls from +16.2% to about +13%, and the three-year return from +33.4% to about +30%, with the earnings part reduced and the multiple part (-6.9% at the 14.8x guideline against 15.9x today) unchanged.

Where I disagree with the first or second set, and why

  • The Exosens dividend (second set, P-02): there is none, so net financial items are heavier. Changed.
  • Tax is "not a leak" (first set, C-54) and 23% to 24% (second set, P-08): the H1 2026 arithmetic says the underlying rate was near 30%, and the mix moves to France and Germany. Changed to 25%, with the FY2026 results on 19 March 2027 as the test; each point is 0.02 EUR.
  • The reported-EPS tax effect on the marks (second set): none, under Cyprus law; reported EPS swings more than the second set said, and a fall in Exosens' price gets no tax relief.
  • The 3.5x covenant (first and second sets): supported by ING, but the December 2025 information memorandum's 3.00:1 on the revolving facility is unresolved. The first set's 10% probability of a covenant-forced equity raise inside 18 months I split into about 20% that the covenant forces an action, of which half is an Exosens stake sale (liquid, tax-free in Cyprus, about 283 EURm) and half a discounted equity raise. The stake converts most of the covenant tail from dilution into de-risking, unless Exosens' price falls at the same time.
  • "Tube price locked" on OCCAR (second set, P-04): fixed for ordered volumes, renegotiable on annual placements; the hedge is partial. The second set's -0.6 to -0.8 points on night-vision margin already carries this, so no change.
  • The first set's clean FY2026 EPS of 1.26 to 1.30 EUR against the second set's 1.39 EUR: both were partly right. The clean H1 net income was 37 EURm (first set) because tax was high, not because finance cost was (second set's reading (b)). My 1.35 EUR sits between them for that reason.
  • HGH from February (second set): late March per the interim report; immaterial net of interest, not changed.

The third set: Assessing the predictability of the financial forecasts for the next 18 months

Appendix Forecast inputs the Investment Analyst changed before the memo

Forecast inputs changed by the third and fourth sets: Theon International Plc (THEON-NL), as at 31-Oct-26

Money in millions of EUR, except per-share figures; costs and outflows negative. Only the lines changed are shown; every other line of Parts 1 to 3 of the second set's forecast inputs stands.

Part 1. Profit and loss inputs, one column for each of the five fiscal years

Input FY2026 (year to 31-Dec-26) FY2027 (year to 31-Dec-27) FY2028 (year to 31-Dec-28) FY2029 (year to 31-Dec-29) FY2030 (year to 31-Dec-30)
Net financial items -18 -27 -27 -24 -21

The H1 2026 interim report shows no dividend income from the Exosens stake (the second set netted 3 to 5 EURm a year) and other finance expenses (letters of guarantee, fees) of 3.73 EURm in the half, about 7 EURm a year against the 4 EURm assumed, so from FY2027 net financial items are 2 to 5 EURm a year heavier (gross debt of about 640, 625, 570 and 500 EURm at 3.5% falling to 3.25%, plus 6.5 EURm of other costs, less 1.5 EURm of interest on cash); FY2026 stays at -18 because the H1 actual excluding the marks was about -8.5 EURm, lighter than the second set's -10 EURm midpoint.

Input FY2026 (year to 31-Dec-26) FY2027 (year to 31-Dec-27) FY2028 (year to 31-Dec-28) FY2029 (year to 31-Dec-29) FY2030 (year to 31-Dec-30)
Tax rate (%) 25.0 25.0 25.0 25.0 25.0

The H1 2026 fair-value gain of 37.24 EURm was untaxed (Cyprus exempts gains on listed securities; the only deferred-tax item was a 0.32 EURm credit), so the tax charge fell on operating profit alone and implies an underlying rate near 30% in the half, and the mix is moving to France (HGH, about 25%) and Germany (about 30%); 25% is a blend of that evidence and the company's 23% to 24% guidance, to be tested at the FY2026 results on 19 March 2027, with each point worth about 0.02 EUR a share.

Resulting cross-check (adjusted basis: adjusted EBIT plus net financial items, less tax at 25%, less minorities of 1.5, 2.5, 3.5, 4.5 and 5.5 EURm, over 77.55m, 77.6m, 77.8m, 78.05m and 78.2m average basic shares)

Cross-check FY2026 (year to 31-Dec-26) FY2027 (year to 31-Dec-27) FY2028 (year to 31-Dec-28) FY2029 (year to 31-Dec-29) FY2030 (year to 31-Dec-30)
Earnings per share 1.35 1.63 1.87 2.12 2.31
Net income 104.8 126.7 145.2 165.5 180.6

The model, which has no minorities line, will show net income of about 106.3, 129.2, 148.7, 170.0 and 186.1 EURm and earnings per share of about 1.37, 1.67, 1.91, 2.18 and 2.38 EUR; the differences are the minorities. Reported-basis earnings per share are about 1.45, 1.49, 1.75, 2.00 and 2.21 EUR (non-recurring items, HGH and Merio acquired-intangible amortisation, and the untaxed Exosens marks, which are about +14 EURm in FY2026 and zero thereafter).

Appendix The share-price co-movement table the Investment Analyst was given

Co-movement of THEON-NL with its dashboard peers (computed 2026-10-30 from the master dashboard's daily prices)

What this is: daily close-to-close returns over rolling windows of 126 trading days (about six months). Correlation: how closely the daily moves line up (1 = always together, 0 = unrelated). Beta: how far the stock moves for a 1% move in the basket. R-squared: the share of the stock's daily price variance the peer basket explains; the remainder is the stock's own (idiosyncratic) movement. Basket: the peers below, equal-weighted. Prices only; the multiple's own history is in the financials block.

Peers (from the dashboard data file): Montana Aerospace (AERO-CH), Scandinavian Astor (ASTOR-SE), Exail Technologies (EXA-FR), Exosens (EXENS-FR), Hensoldt (HAG-DE), Kongsberg Gruppen (KOG-NO), MilDef (MILDEF-SE), H&K (MLHK-FR), Melrose Industries (MRO-GB), Ovzon (OVZON-SE)

Appendix Research requests the Investment Analyst raised for the memo

Research requests for the next set

Company: Theon International Plc (THEON-NL, Euronext Amsterdam), a Greek-founded, founder-controlled maker of man-portable night-vision goggles (93% of FY2025 revenue) and thermal imaging systems for armed forces, expanding into platform-based optronics (Kappa Optronics; the PHYLAX system for Rheinmetall), gimbals (Merio, completed October 2026; a Safran joint venture), multi-domain surveillance and counter-drone systems (HGH Systèmes Infrarouges, closing expected late Q1 2027), image intensifier tubes (65.22% of Harder Digital) and AI (Twin Prime), and holding a 9.8% stake in its main tube supplier, Exosens.

Request: OWN-1

Question: For Theon International (THEON-NL), what would a sale or monetisation of its 9.8% stake in Exosens involve and what would it achieve: the contractual constraints from the January 2026 purchase from HLD at EUR 54 a share (lock-up, orderly-market, right-of-first-refusal or standstill undertakings, and any link to the long-term tube supply agreement extended to 2030 on 3 December 2025), the regulatory constraints on a disposal of a stake in a French defence supplier, the Cyprus tax treatment of a disposal by the Cyprus-resident parent, the routes available (block trade, placement, exchangeable bond, collar or margin loan) and their typical discounts for a stake of this size and liquidity, what management and the covering brokers have said about monetising "liquid financial assets" since the H1 2026 results of 7 September 2026, and what precedents of listed defence or industrial companies selling strategic minority stakes to reduce acquisition leverage (for example Leonardo's sales of Leonardo DRS shares, Airbus and Dassault Aviation, Safran and other European examples) show about timing after the leverage peak, the discount achieved and the share-price reaction?

Request: OWN-2

Question: For Theon International (THEON-NL), what is the composition and likely path of its effective income-tax rate: the statutory rates in each jurisdiction where it earns profit (Cyprus for the parent, Greece for Theon Sensors, Germany for Harder Digital and Kappa Optronics, Belgium, France for Merio and HGH Systèmes Infrarouges, South Korea and the Gulf), the reported effective rates of 22.9% in FY2024, 23.8% in FY2025 and 19.9% in H1 2026 (a half in which the EUR 37.24m fair-value gain on the Exosens stake was exempt from tax under Cyprus rules, per the interim report of 24 September 2026), the Greek Development Law 4887/2022 investment exemption, non-deductible items such as acquisition costs and share-based pay, withholding taxes on intra-group dividends, the open Greek tax audit for 2025, the company's stated guidance of 23% to 24%, and how the consolidation of HGH and Merio from 2027 and the growth of the German subsidiaries would move the group rate; and how the effective tax rates of Exosens, Hensoldt, MilDef and Exail Technologies compare and why?

Request: OWN-3

Question: For Theon International (THEON-NL), what are the financial covenants and definitions that govern its bank debt after the EUR 325m syndicated senior unsecured term loan of 7 September 2026 coordinated by Alpha Bank (reported by Global Capital on 9 September 2026 as on tighter terms than the 2025 facility): the leverage ceiling and any step-downs, the interest-cover test, whether the EUR 300m revolving facility of October 2025 (described in the company's information memorandum of 1 December 2025 as carrying a net leverage covenant of 3.00:1 and interest cover of 4.00:1, at Euribor plus 1.5%) was amended or refinanced alongside the term loan, how net debt is defined (treatment of leases, of listed financial assets such as the Exosens stake, and of cash held in subsidiaries), how EBITDA is defined (pro forma inclusion of acquired businesses such as HGH and Merio, treatment of non-recurring items and joint-venture income), the test dates, the margin grid and the interest-rate swaps in place, as disclosed by the company, by the arranging and participating banks, by loan-market reporting or by the brokers; and how these terms compare with Exosens' June 2026 credit agreement (a 3.0x covenant and a 0.95% to 1.75% margin grid) and with other European mid-cap defence acquisition facilities?
